- **Runs never left hanging** `[shipped 2026-10-04]`, a reply that stops making progress now ends, says why, and can be continued, instead of sitting on "thinking" for hours (the 2026-10-04 incident: two overnight Claude-subscription chats went quiet mid-tool and were found the next morning still "running"). Four safeguards, in both loops (the TS loop and the Rust daemon): **a stream idle limit**, a model response that sends nothing for five minutes (Codex's default too; `NEKKO_STREAM_IDLE_MS` changes it, 0 disables) is cut and counts as a transient failure, and the daemon's HTTP client also gives up on a connection that does not open in 30 s; **transient failures are sent again**, an overloaded provider (529), a rate limit (429), a 5xx, a dropped connection or a stalled stream gets up to five attempts with backoff (1 s doubling to 30 s, the provider's `retry-after` honoured, jitter so parallel chats spread out), each attempt regenerating the step from scratch, and the chat shows **Retrying (2 of 5, in 4 s: anthropic 529 …)** in its step rail while the partial text of the failed attempt is dropped; anything else (a 401, a 400 about the request) ends the reply as before, and Stop ends a backoff at once. **The tool request is checkpointed before the tools run** (`step` event), so a shell command, MCP call or sub-agent that never returns leaves the assistant message that asked for it on disk rather than losing the step. **A chat the app was closed on knows it**: the session carries `activeRun` while a turn runs; a host that starts and finds one appends an interrupted note ("The app closed while this reply was running") and the chat opens on a **Reply interrupted** notice with **Continue** / **Start over**, read from the transcript itself (any transcript ending on an unanswered tool call or an `interrupted` reply shows it), where before a restart showed a silently cut-off chat with no way to resume. Also: a daemon-driven run whose events stop is probed (`loop:alive`) every 30 s and ended as interrupted when the daemon restarted or cannot be reached; a run the daemon drives for a host that has since restarted still gets its checkpoints saved; Stop on a parent chat stops the sub-agents it spawned (they used to run on, and the parent's turn waited for them); streamable-HTTP MCP calls time out after ten minutes.
- **No tool-step limit** `[shipped 2026-10-03]`, replies have no tool-step limit at all; the concept was removed (no setting, no budget option in the TS loop or the Rust daemon, no `step_limit` stop). A reply runs until the model stops calling tools, a safeguard trips, a stream fails, or the user presses Stop. Loop detection nudges repeated identical calls/results and ends a reply that stays stuck through a tools-withheld wrap-up; output and repetition guards remain active. The host hands a run to the engine daemon only when `daemon:info` advertises `loop:unbounded`, so an older daemon that still reads a budget can never cap a reply. Reply observation records stop reasons and tool-step counts locally without content, excluding incognito chats. Spend and wall-clock budgets remain future work (TASKS.md).
- **Chat control modes** `[shipped]`, per-chat Ask / Guardrails / YOLO, plus Offline (local-only, no tools/connectors/internet) and Incognito (no transcript/memory persistence); in-composer Tools popover to enable/disable builtins + MCP tools.
- **Competitor-parity chat features** `[shipped]`, export to Markdown, regenerate last response, edit & resend, per-message + code-block Copy, slash-command palette (`/`), @-mention files, chat-list search + rename, conversation tags, per-chat ⋯ menu (Pin/Rename/Delete).
- **Command Center: the wall** `[reworked 2026-10-03]` `[split tree 2026-10-04]`, every agent and terminal as a **live window on one wall**, the same chat and terminal panes the Agent tab shows, so work is handled on the wall (answer a question, approve a command, type the next instruction) rather than read off a card. The wall is arranged by the **same split tree the Agent tab uses** (up to 8 across and 8 down): every two windows share a **draggable divider** (pointer capture, so the drag keeps tracking over a terminal), every window has the **split compass** (hover its **+**, pick a side, pick what goes there: chat, terminal, Automations or Insights), and dragging a window's strip onto any side of another moves it there, onto the middle trades their places. **Automations** and **Insights** are windows like the rest: placed and sized by hand, removed with their ×, brought back from the toolbar's **Add window** menu, which also offers a new agent, a new terminal, and the chats and shells already running that are not on the wall yet. A new agent nobody pointed at a side for (auto-add or the toolbar) appends in **reading order** and re-tiles the wall into balanced rows, rather than halving the last agent; explicit compass placement retains its chosen split. Unpositioned terminals retain largest-window placement; **Auto-arrange** re-tiles everything into even rows and columns. The toolbar also holds the **filter** (All / Agents / Terminals; the panels always stay) and the **Auto-add new agents** switch (every chat that starts, spawned sub-agents included, joins the wall; completing a chat takes it off). **One composer for the whole wall** `[2026-10-05]`: the agent windows show only their transcripts (and anything waiting on you), and a single composer panel speaks for the **selected** agent, carrying that chat's own controls (model, mode, tools, image settings for an image chat, the queue, the question it is asking). Clicking an agent window selects it; **Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab** walk the agent windows in reading order and **Ctrl+1…9** (or Alt) pick one by the number on its strip. The selected window wears the accent ring and a tinted strip, and a faint **tether** in the accent runs from the composer to the wall's edge under (or over) that window, so the composer visibly belongs to an agent; terminals, Automations and Insights are never connected. The composer docks to one of **six places** (above or below the windows; left, centred or right), chosen from the picker in its strip, and the choice is part of the wall setting. `[fixed 2026-10-12; unit-tested, measured in an isolated fixture, and checked by hand in the running desktop app]` Its **dragged width and height are part of the wall setting too**, so a resized composer is the same size after visiting another page or restarting the app (they used to live in the component and reset whenever the Agents page was left); a double-click on the grip still returns to the default, and walls saved before this load at the default size. `[2026-10-13]` The default is **two lines of text**: undragged, the composer is as tall as its content with a two-line editor (it was a fixed 320 px), and a resize made just before leaving the page or closing the app is saved too (it used to wait out a 600 ms settle delay that leaving cancelled, and the next load preferred the host's older copy). Dragged as wide as it goes, the composer lines up **edge to edge with the windows above it**: its widest is the width of the windows' stage, and its row is inset like the wall (14 px, plus the scrollbar gutter the wall reserves on the right), where before it could overshoot them by a few pixels on one side and stop short on the other. A saved width wider than the wall now (a smaller window, the panels opened) is drawn at the wall's width and keeps its saved value. With no agent on the wall it offers to start one. **Status is right on the first frame:** the wall (and the Agent tab's sidebar) start from the chats the app already knows to be mid-turn and confirm with the engine on opening, so an agent that is working never shows as idle for a second first. **Needs you stands out:** a window waiting on an approval or a question wears a warm ring and a tinted strip, with **no duplicate attention ribbon** above the wall; approvals and questions remain actionable on the agent window or selected-agent composer. A phone-width wall stacks its windows one above the other. The wall is **a setting** (`commandWall`), so the desktop, web and phone editions of one install show the same wall; the browser keeps a copy (`localStorage`, `nekko.commandWall`) as the fast first paint and the fallback, and the previous grid's `nekko.commandGrid` is carried over as a tiled wall the first time. A divider also moves with the **arrow keys** once focused (it is in the tab order), on the wall and in the Agent tab alike; a fresh install seeds it with the chats touched in the last day and the shells still running, tiled, with the two panels in a column on the right. An empty wall shows the line-art placeholder. The **Insights** window is made of the panels ticked in its gear (Vitals: agents working · waiting on you · automations active · terminals live · tokens today · est. spend plus fleet-by-type counts; Optimize tips; Cost; Tokens over time; By model; Replies; Services), flowing into as many columns as the window is wide. **Automations** lists every scheduled / recurring / background task as a schedule row (kind glyph, cadence, live "running now" / **"next in Xm" countdown** / paused / error state, run count + last-run time + last-result snippet, with Run-now / Pause-Resume / Delete on hover). Per-GPU VRAM lives in the global **monitor chip** (see Resource monitors). This replaced the 2026-10-03 auto/fixed grid (which could only resize whole rows and columns and kept the panels above or below the wall) and, before it, the 2026-07-25 three-lane board of cards. In the rail the **Agent tab sits above Command Center** (a saved rail order still wins).
- **Agent types & fleet view** `[shipped]`, every running or scheduled agent is given a **type** derived from what it's doing, the skill in play, the task kind/condition, and the prompt, so a team can tell a **code review bot** from a **production monitor** from a **research agent** or **build agent** at a glance. Types show as coloured badges on agent cards and task cards, and a **Fleet** panel at the top of the Command Center groups everything in flight by type with live counts. This turns the dashboard into a team-wide monitor: what's running, what kind of work it is, and where to step in. Classification is a pure, unit-tested heuristic (`classifyAgent`/`classifySession` in `shared/agent-types.ts`).
- **Per-agent queues** `[shipped]`, each chat agent has a **run-queue**: line up follow-up prompts (from the composer's **Queue** button, even while a turn is streaming) and the agent works through them one after another when the current turn finishes. Queued items show on the composer and on the agent's Command Center card, each removable. Combined with scheduled / recurring / background tasks, the Command Center becomes a single place to see everything each agent has done, is doing, and has queued next.
- **Optimization insights** `[shipped]`, an **Optimize** panel on the Command Center turns your own usage into prioritized, actionable tips (warnings first, then by estimated saving): route light chats to a connected local model, use a smaller model for short chats (with a concrete cheaper alternative), prune context when input dwarfs output, the single biggest cost driver, and pinning your go-to models. Pure heuristics over the existing usage summary + sessions + providers, no extra model calls; shows a total potential-savings figure.
- **Cost breakdowns** `[shipped]`, a Command Center **Cost** panel: this-month **actual** spend + a month-end **projection**, an **all-time** total, a **daily-spend chart**, a **per-agent breakdown** (accurate to the model each session used), and a collapsible **token-pricing reference** (USD / 1M tokens, published list prices). Local models are $0; everything is labelled an estimate. Computed in the host from the usage log (`totalCost` / `bySessionCost` / per-day `cost`).
- **Automation tasks** `[shipped]`, **scheduled** (run once at a time), **recurring** (every interval), and **background** (long-running agents that stay alive **forever** or **until a condition** the agent judges met) tasks, shown in the Command Center's **Tasks & scheduled work** board (cadence, status, run count, last result; Run-now / Pause-Resume / Open-chat / Delete; live updates). A host **scheduler** fires due tasks through the normal agent loop, reusing one chat session per task so background agents keep context. Created from a chat via the **⚡ Automate** menu (pre-filled with the chat's project/model/draft).
- **Skills in the `/` menu** `[shipped]`, the composer's `/` menu lists **skills**, standard capabilities any agent can run (research, plan, review, security-review, simplify, test, explain, fix, commit, pr), above the user's saved prompts. Invoking a skill attaches it as a compact usage chip in the composer and in the sent message instead of dumping its template into the text box; the full instructions are still expanded into the prompt sent to the model. `/research` relies on the tools Nekko Agent actually has (`fetch_url`, and the browser where it is available) and does not assume a web search engine; `/pr` follows the repository's own PR conventions when it has them and always includes an explicit **Unfinished work / release blockers** section, as the shared system prompt requires. **`goal` is a highlighted (★) skill**: `/goal <condition>` starts a long-running **background agent** that works until the condition is met (a real "work until done" loop), surfaced in the Tasks board.

- **Skills tab & workflow visualizer** `[shipped]`, a dedicated **Skills** view in the left rail. Skills are grouped by category (Research & planning, Code quality, Delivery, Automation) in a searchable list; selecting one shows its description, the tools it uses, and a **workflow visualizer** that renders the skill's steps as an n8n / Make-style node graph (trigger → context → agent → tool → decision/loop → output) on a dotted canvas, with colour-coded node types, bezier connectors, branch labels, and dashed return arrows for loops (e.g. `/goal`'s "goal met? → no → keep working" cycle, `/research`'s fan-out to parallel source reads). A **Use in chat** button adds the skill as a usage chip in a chat composer. The graph layout is a pure, unit-tested layered algorithm in `shared/skills.ts` (`layoutWorkflow`), so each skill carries a real `workflow` definition rather than ad-hoc art. The canvas is also a **teaching surface**: an explainer panel beside it walks the steps in run order ("How this skill runs"), and clicking any node, on the canvas or in the list, explains that step: what it does, what its node kind means (trigger / context / agent / tool / decision / loop / output), and where its branches and loops go ("yes → Done", "no: loops back to Do the work").

- **Vaizer skills hub** `[shipped]`, the Marketplace has a **Vaizer** shelf connected to the public skills marketplace ([nekko-labs/vaizer](https://github.com/nekko-labs/vaizer), browsable at vaizer.app/skills). An optional integration in the same spirit as the app's others: the shelf renders **fully offline** from a bundled catalog snapshot (or the last cached fetch), and only touches the network when the user clicks **Refresh** (pulls the live catalog) or installs (fetches the skill's real, current SKILL.md so file-based installs get the verbatim skill, not a summary). Skills carry Vaizer's **trust tiers** (🟣 official, built + reviewed by Nekko Labs; 🟢 community, audit before use, with an in-app caution note) and install one-click into Nekko Agent, Claude Code, or Codex like any marketplace skill; those installs snapshot the skill onto the install record so they stay runnable offline and after catalog changes.
- **Skills marketplace** `[shipped]`, the Skills tab has a **Marketplace** with three shelves: **Installed** (what you added, and where each copy lives), **Nekko Labs** (first-party skills: review-council, spec-sync, changelog, standup, dep-audit, a11y-audit, i18n-sweep), and **Popular online** (our own short versions of well-known ecosystem skills: brainstorm and systematic-debug after obra/superpowers, pdf/docx/xlsx after Anthropic's document skills, conventional-commits after the Conventional Commits spec; ranked by the original repository's GitHub stars, a dated snapshot rather than a live count; the catalog ships as a curated offline-first snapshot). `[attribution updated 2026-10-09]` Every catalog entry's text is written by Nekko Labs and credited to Nekko Labs; a skill that summarises someone else's work names the original only as a reference ("based on", linked on the card and in the exported SKILL.md footer) and copies none of its text. Anthropic's document skills are proprietary and are never downloaded or bundled. Unverified: the card's "based on" link has not been checked in the running app. A skill can be **installed into Nekko Agent** (it joins the composer's `/` menu and the Skills library) or **exported to other agent apps** that read the SKILL.md convention, Claude Code (`~/.claude/skills`) and Codex (`~/.codex/skills`), with per-target availability detection, safe no-clobber installs, and one-click removal. Every marketplace skill renders in the same workflow visualizer (bespoke graphs for the flagship skills, a derived graph otherwise) plus a "what it tells the agent" instructions card. **Full instructions in-app** `[implemented 2026-10-09; not yet verified in the running app]`: a marketplace skill installed into Nekko Agent sends the model its full instructions, not just its one-line template, matching what imported skills already do. A Vaizer skill sends the body of the SKILL.md it was installed with (frontmatter stripped); a catalog skill sends its `instructions`. The prompt is a short "Use the <name> skill" line, the instructions, then the skill's `template` as the task, with the user's input after it. A skill with nothing beyond its template (e.g. a Vaizer skill installed offline, whose instructions are just its description) sends the template alone. This applies everywhere an installed skill runs: the composer's `/` menu and + menu, the Marketplace's "Use in chat" button, and workflow skill steps. The composer's skill chip and the Context Inspector count tokens over that same prompt. Both **Use in chat** buttons (Skills library and Marketplace) attach the skill as the usage chip in the active chat's composer, the same way picking it from the `/` menu does, and leave any unsent draft untouched; with no usable chat they open or create one first. They no longer paste the skill's prompt into the text box (the goal skill, which has no chip, still drops `/goal ` into the composer). Covered by `apps/desktop/src/renderer/store-attachSkill.test.ts`; not yet clicked through in the app. Verified by unit tests (`packages/core/src/skills-market.test.ts`, `packages/core/src/vaizer.test.ts`); a run from the `/` menu in the app is still to do.
- **Skills from GitHub** `[implemented 2026-10-14; unit-tested, checked in an isolated web edition (light, desktop width); dark/phone and token search unverified]`, the Marketplace finds Agent Skills (agentskills.io layout: a folder with `SKILL.md`) on GitHub instead of copying them into our catalog. **User intent:** many more skills without the bundled catalog growing, and without bundling other people's text. **Three ways in:** (1) **Add from GitHub**: paste a skill folder URL (`github.com/owner/repo/tree/<ref>/<path>`, a `blob` or raw `SKILL.md` URL) or a bare `owner/repo`; the app lists what it finds and nothing is installed until the user picks a skill and an install target. (2) **GitHub shelves**: repositories shown as Marketplace shelves, defaulting to `anthropics/skills`, `obra/superpowers` and `addyosmani/agent-skills` (`DEFAULT_SKILL_SOURCES`), editable from the Marketplace (**Keep as a shelf** after a lookup, **✕** on a shelf) and stored as `settings.skillSources`; removing a shelf leaves installed skills installed. (3) **Search GitHub** (Enter in the Marketplace search): with a GitHub token it searches `SKILL.md` files and lists individual skills; without one GitHub refuses code search, so it searches repositories (`topic:agent-skills` first) and says why, each hit offering **List skills**. Typing in the search box always filters loaded shelves locally first. **Network boundary:** nothing is fetched until the user clicks Load / Refresh / Find / Search / List skills, opens a GitHub skill (its `SKILL.md` is read to show the instructions) or installs; a loaded shelf is cached in `skill-sources.json` and shown from there afterwards (`cached`), a failed refresh keeps the cached list and shows `could not refresh`. The GitHub token (Connectors' GitHub PAT, else `GH_TOKEN` / `GITHUB_TOKEN`) is sent only to `api.github.com`, never to raw file hosts. Without a token GitHub allows 60 API requests an hour; a shelf costs two or three plus one per skill folder that ships its own LICENSE. **Install:** always the author's verbatim, current `SKILL.md` from its repository (never a rewrite), through the same `installSkill` payload path as Vaizer, so the existing name check, no-clobber rule for Claude Code / Codex folders and offline record snapshot apply; ids are `gh-<owner>-<repo>-<folder>` and the card credits the repository owner. **Trust and licensing:** every GitHub skill is shown as unreviewed, with a caution that it runs with the machine's permissions; its detected license is shown (a folder's own LICENSE file beats the repository's); a skill whose license reserves all rights (Anthropic's document skills: docx, pdf, pptx, xlsx) is listed **link only** and cannot be installed. A skill folder that ships scripts or references is flagged: only `SKILL.md` is installed, so steps relying on those files may not work. A `SKILL.md` over 256 KB, without instructions, with an invalid name, or a URL with `.` / `..` segments (which would otherwise resolve to a different repository) is refused. **Unchanged:** the bundled catalog policy above (our own text, originals only linked) and the Vaizer shelf. **Not done:** no background refresh of GitHub shelves (the Settings → Updates skills check still covers Vaizer only), no update-available notice for installed GitHub skills, bundled skill files besides `SKILL.md` are not installed, and the CLI / MCP `skills install` do not accept GitHub URLs. Covered by `packages/shared/src/skill-sources.test.ts` and `packages/host/src/skill-sources.test.ts` (a fake GitHub, no network); a live load of obra/superpowers, an install of its `brainstorming` skill and the docx link-only case were checked by hand in an isolated web edition; published screenshots are still to do.
- **Imported Agent Skills** `[implemented; Skills tab and / menu UI visually unverified]`, Nekko Agent reads the Agent Skills other tools already keep on this machine (folders holding a `SKILL.md` with `name` / `description` frontmatter, per agentskills.io) so a user's Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI or `.agents` skills run with any model. **Scanned folders**: `.claude/skills`, `.codex/skills`, `.gemini/skills` and `.agents/skills`, both in the home directory (scope `user`) and in each workspace folder (scope `project`). **Precedence**: built-in and marketplace skill names are reserved and always win; a project skill wins a name collision over a user skill; folders Nekko Agent exported itself from the Marketplace are not imported back. A `SKILL.md` over 256 KB, an invalid name, or an empty body is skipped. **Read-only**: the files are read and never written, moved, or deleted; the Skills tab lists them under an **Imported** category with a source/scope badge (e.g. "Claude Code · user") and the skill's folder path, without the marketplace "installed" label or any edit/uninstall action. **Invocation in this phase is user-only**: imported skills join the composer's `/` menu and the + menu's skill flyout, and the list is rescanned each time the `/` menu opens (as well as at app start and on the Skills tab), so a skill added while the app runs appears without a restart. The prompt sent to the model names the skill folder only when the skill ships files besides `SKILL.md` (scripts, references), and says that folder is not the user's project; a skill with only `SKILL.md` gets just its instructions, because small local models otherwise mistook the skill folder for the project. Verified by unit tests (`packages/host/src/external-skills.test.ts`) and a manual run of a `~/.claude/skills` test skill from the `/` menu; before/after screenshots of the Skills tab and `/` menu are still to be captured. **Known limits**: tools stay confined to the chat's workspace, so a skill whose bundled scripts must run from its own folder may not complete; small local models follow skill instructions less reliably than frontier models. **Open decisions**: whether imported skills are on by default or behind a setting, and whether file tools may read a skill's own folder (today a chat without a project folder can read it, a chat with one cannot).
- **Model-invoked imported skills** `[planned]`, a catalog of imported skills' names and descriptions in the system prompt plus a loading tool, so the model can pick and load a skill itself instead of waiting for `/name`. Not implemented; depends on the skill-folder read-access decision above.

### Files, editor & visual surfaces (IDE-like, "stay in the app")

- **Built-in file viewer/editor** `[shipped, editing pass 2026-07-28]`, clicking a file (or a spec/plan/tasks row, or a tree entry) opens it as a **window** in the current workspace, splittable on any side of a chat or terminal, never handed off to the OS. Markdown renders as a **document** (a real heading scale, reflowed paragraphs, tables, task-list checkboxes, embedded HTML reduced to its text, images and relative links as chips that open the real file) with a Source/Preview toggle. Other text opens in a lightweight mono editor that behaves like an editor: **auto-save** on an idle pause (toggleable, remembered, flushed when the window closes and on quit), **undo/redo** with its own history (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z, coalescing a typing run into one step), **copy / cut / paste** actions beside the shortcuts, Tab-to-indent, Ctrl/Cmd-S, a save-state readout (Saving shortly → Saved), and binary/oversized-file guards. Fixes the old dead-click where `.md` files silently failed to open (`shell.openPath` with no registered handler).
- **Integrated browser pane** `[shipped]`, a Chromium `<webview>` pane with a URL bar (back / forward / reload / open-external) for previewing a local dev server or docs right next to the code that drives them (code-left, preview-right).
- **File explorer, inside the right panel** `[shipped, reworked 2026-07-28]`, the file tree lives in the right-hand panel's **Folders** section rather than in a side-pane of its own: each project folder is an accordion, and expanding one reveals its lazy-loaded VS Code–style tree with color-coded file-type icons. Clicking a file opens it in a tab and edits in-app. Choosing what the agent can see and browsing what's there are the same act, so they're the same control. (Superseded the separate dockable **Files** side-pane, which duplicated the Folders list one pane over.) Not a full IDE, no language servers, debugger, or Monaco.
- **Diff & approval (Devin-style)** `[shipped]`, every file the agent touches this session is snapshotted on first write; a **Changes** panel (a `Δ N` button in the chat header) shows a client-side line diff and lets you **keep / revert** at **line**, **file**, or **all-files** granularity, live-updating as the agent edits. Writes still happen immediately (the tool loop is never gated); "reject" reverts.
- **Hoverable Context Inspector** `[shipped]`, every Context Inspector section and source explains itself on hover (what it is, why it's included, how to control it), turning the provenance panel into a teaching surface.
- **Prompt analyzer** `[shipped]`, a live, fully client-side (no LLM, zero-latency, offline) composer analyzer that identifies the parts of a prompt (role / task / context / examples / output-format / constraints / tone / variables…), underlines weak spots inline, gives an **A–F health score**, and recommends a model tier (fast / balanced / frontier), a marketable always-on coaching surface. Missing-part chips are **click-to-fill**: clicking **+ Role / + Task / + Context / + Examples / + Format / + Constraints** inserts a starter snippet matched to what the prompt is about (code / writing / data, question vs. task) via instant offline text matching, role at the top of the draft, everything else appended. An opt-in LLM "Improve prompt" rewrite (before/after diff) and model-generated part fills are deferred.
- **Inline editor comments** `[shipped]`, in the file editor, a **+** appears on each line (a scroll-synced gutter); clicking it opens an inline comment box. The comment is captured with its file + line context and the agent picks it up via one of two actions: **Add to prompt** (queues the comment into the composer for the next turn, so several annotations batch into one ask) or **Run now** (dispatches it immediately to the active chat). A line keeps a gutter marker (persisted in `comments.json`) until resolved; a bottom dock lists a line's comments with re-send / resolve. Lets you review code in place and turn margin notes into agent work without leaving the file.
- **Design board (Figma-style)** `[shipped]`, a **Design** tab that lays out the app's UI pages as snapshots on a zoomable board, like a Figma canvas. Live-page snapshots are **read-only live previews**, each card is a scaled, non-interactive `<iframe>` of the page (so it mirrors the app as it actually renders, no capture infra). Click a card to attach **persistent notes** (saved in `design.json`, survive across sessions) or **comments** that feed the prompt, the same **Add to prompt / Run now** actions as inline editor comments. As the agent edits the UI, previews **reload so you watch the design update live**; a per-page **"updating" indicator** flags an in-flight change, and clicking it jumps to the driving agent's chat pane. A visual companion to the file/diff surfaces: see the app as pages, not just files.
- **Design studio (sketch → prototype, prompt → design)** `[shipped]`, the Design tab's creation layer, a Claude Design-style loop. **Draw it**: a pointer-events sketch canvas (Apple Pencil / touch friendly, `touch-action: none`, DPR-sharp, pen colors/sizes/eraser/undo) whose drawing plus an optional note goes to a vision model and comes back as a working prototype. **Describe it**: a prompt studio (with starter idea chips) that generates a design one-shot. Concepts render on the board via iframe `srcdoc`, are **mirrored into the workspace as real files** (`nekko-designs/<slug>.html`), and carry a **Refine** box in their side sheet that regenerates the prototype from a follow-up instruction while keeping the rest intact. Generation runs host-side (`design:generate`) with the default provider/model, one-shot, no agent loop; concept comments route to the agent with the prototype's file path so agent iteration works on the real file.

### Model training & goals (the data-scientist agent)

- **Training tab** `[shipped]`, train a model in a simple UI (purpose + dataset + base model) with full expert levers (framework, epochs, batch, lr, metric + direction, experiment/time budgets, free-form constraints). Datasets from Hugging Face, Kaggle, local paths, or URLs; base models from Hugging Face or local. A data-scientist agent runs the actual experiments in a dedicated chat session (hidden from the regular chat boards); the dashboard shows a one-line explainer of what a run does, a live stat tiles row (best score, experiments, success rate, emergent niches, self-repairs, runtime, turns), a current-champion card, and the **idea maze** (every idea tried, growing over time, path-to-leader highlighted, click a node for details). A **live "now" strip** calls out what the run is doing right this moment (an explicit "starting up…" state the instant you hit start, then the currently-running experiment plus the latest activity line), and starting a run takes you straight to its dashboard. An **Artifacts card** shows exactly what the run produced (the trained model plus each harness file), where each lives, and how to use it, with every path and the run's output folder openable in the OS file manager. Run controls are grouped as one cluster (Start · Pause · Stop) set apart from Open chat / Delete. **Harness output**: a run can produce an agent file / skill / spec beside the model for its use case. The form also takes an optional **data-scientist model** override (the model running the experiments, distinct from the model being trained; App default otherwise).
- **Goal runs (plan-first)** `[shipped; the Goals tab became Workflows 2026-08-13]`, long-running goal solving for real work, not model testing: the agent **plans first** (an ordered execution plan of concrete, verifiable steps, written before any execution), **executes** step by step (statuses + one-line outcome notes, done only when verified), and **iterates** (revises the plan when reality disagrees) until the goal is finished. Dashboard built for hours/days legibility: "working for" clock, phase chip (planning/executing/verifying/finished), the live plan checklist with progress bar, current-step card, goal-shaped stat tiles, and the activity feed. Runs auto-resume after a host restart. The dedicated Goals **tab** is gone (that nav slot is now Workflows, which covers repeatable procedures rather than one-off goals); goal runs are listed alongside training runs in the **Training** tab so any that already exist stay reachable, and `/goal` in the composer still starts a background goal task.
- **Mid-run guidance** `[shipped]`, both tabs accept hints, new approaches, or new-data pointers at any time; unconsumed hints queue as chips and fold into the agent's next turn.
- **report_experiment tool** `[shipped]`, run-driven sessions get an extra builtin tool the agent uses to register/update experiments (id, parent lineage, approach family, status incl. self-repair, score, metric, note); the host tracks the leader and replies with the current standing so the agent always knows what to beat.
- **report_artifact tool** `[shipped]`, run-driven sessions also get a tool to register the concrete outputs a run produces (kind: model / agent-file / skill / spec / dataset / code / report / other, plus a title, workspace-relative path, and how-to-use note). Upserts by path; the host logs the first sighting as a milestone. This drives the dashboard's **Artifacts card** so a finished run answers "what did I get, and where is it?" at a glance instead of leaving it buried in the log. The agent brief instructs the model to call it the moment each artifact (the trained model + every harness file) exists on disk, and to keep everything under the run's `nekko-training/<slug>` (or `nekko-goal/<slug>`) output folder.
- **update_plan tool** `[shipped]`, goal-run sessions also get a plan tool: replace=true writes/rewrites the ordered plan, upserts keep step statuses (pending/active/done/skipped) and notes current. The host logs step completions as milestones, drives the goal-run checklist live, and echoes the plan back so the agent always knows each step's id.

### Workflows (procedural automation)

- **Workflows tab** `[shipped 2026-08-13, replaced the Goals tab]`, GitHub-Actions-style automation where a step is an **agent prompt**, an installed **skill**, another **workflow**, or a **shell command**. Steps are **routed, not just sequenced**: each declares where the run goes on success and on failure, and a route may point back at an earlier step, so build → verify → back to build is a first-class shape (bounded by a per-step re-entry cap so a non-converging workflow still terminates and says why). Steps carry retries, a timeout, and continue-on-error. Agent steps end with a **PASS/FAIL token** so they can be routed like a shell exit code, and every agent step in a run shares one chat session so later steps see earlier work. Each run records every attempt (step, attempt number, outcome, output/error, and a link to the step's transcript); history is capped per workflow, and a run interrupted by a quit or crash is written off as interrupted on the next boot rather than looking stuck.
- **Triggers** `[shipped]`, per workflow, any number of: **manual**, **schedule** (a 5-field cron expression, parsed in shared so the editor validates it and previews its next fire, or a plain every-N-minutes interval), **CLI** (`nekko-agent workflow run <name>`; `nekko-agent workflow trigger <command>` fires everything listening for that command), **Slack** (channel + optional keyword), and **git provider** events (PR opened / updated / closed / merged / commented / reviewed, push, tag, issue opened / commented) filtered by repo and branch globs. Provider events are **normalized across GitHub, GitLab, Bitbucket and other**, so a workflow reacts to "a PR opened" regardless of whose vocabulary the webhook used. Every transport enters through one host door (`workflow:event`), which in the web edition is reachable as an HTTP endpoint for a Slack app or a webhook, so all of them match by the same rules.
- **Built for hundreds** `[shipped]`, the list filters by search (reaching into step bodies, not just names), enabled state, trigger kind, and category, and groups either **by category** or **by event listener**, a workflow appearing under every event it listens for. Groups collapse and cap their rows with a "show more", so a few hundred workflows stay navigable.
- **Step canvas** `[shipped]`, expanding a row (or opening the editor) draws the step graph as a node canvas in the same layered-node visual language as the Skills tab (the layout code is now shared by both). Failure routes are drawn in the danger color, loop-backs dip under the row as dashed edges, and terminal Done / Failed nodes close the graph. With a run selected the same canvas shows that run's per-step outcome and attempt count, so the shape of a workflow and how it last behaved are one picture.
- **Editing** `[shipped]`, create from a **template** (build/verify loop, PR reviewer, nightly maintenance sweep, or empty) or from scratch; the editor redraws the canvas as you type, warns about steps nothing routes to, and re-points any route left dangling by a deleted step. Workflows can be duplicated (arriving disabled, so two don't answer the same trigger by accident), enabled/disabled, and deleted (which takes the run history with it).
- **Integration triggers** `[shipped 2026-09-08]`, beyond manual/schedule/CLI, a workflow can fire from the user's connected apps: **connector triggers** poll the configured Linear, Jira, Slack, Discord, or Teams account on an interval for new matching events (polling is the default because a desktop app behind NAT can't receive inbound hooks), and a **webhook trigger** gives each workflow its own secreted URL (`POST /api/hooks/<slug>`) on the server/Docker editions, with an opt-in loopback listener on desktop. Every external event still enters through the single `workflow:event` door, so matching rules stay identical however the event arrived.
- **Action steps** `[shipped 2026-09-08]`, a fifth step kind that pushes results back out through the integrations: post a Slack or Teams message, comment on or create Linear/Jira issues, comment on a PR, set a commit status. Fields interpolate run context (`{{trigger.*}}`, `{{steps.x.output}}`, `{{run.status}}`), and the editor offers real pickers (channel, issue, repo) instead of raw payloads.
- **Local CI runner** `[shipped 2026-09-08]`, a workflow template that turns a machine into a private runner for git-host events: on `push`/`pr_opened` it checks out the ref, runs the repo's build/test steps locally, then reports the outcome back as a **commit status** (`nekko-agent/<workflow>`) and, on failure, a PR comment with the output tail. This is how a team spends their own hardware instead of hosted-runner minutes; it deliberately is *not* an implementation of the GitHub Actions runner protocol, and untrusted-PR execution needs its own review before enablement (see Scope Boundaries).

### Context, guardrails & memory (the differentiators)

- **Context Inspector** `[shipped, restructured 2026-07-28]`, the right-hand panel, now **two stacked sections split by a draggable divider**, the way VS Code stacks its sidebar views:
  - **Folders** on top: each project folder is an accordion carrying what the agent may see (primary / supporting / excluded, promote-to-primary, remove) and expanding into that folder's **file tree**, so browsing and opening a file happens where you choose the context. Folders support one primary plus supporting roots, and the selection drives the chat's multi-root context.
  - **Context** below: Sources (attached files, SPEC.md, guideline/memory links) + Breakdown (live per-source token view, toggle/pin, headroom bar). The former Project context section is now **Guidelines**, above the Spec panel, with each guideline and memory entry individually enableable or disableable.
  - The split **gives and takes ground**: with every folder collapsed, Folders is content-height and Context owns the panel; opening a tree hands Folders the height you dragged it to, capped so Context always keeps a usable minimum, and the height you asked for is remembered rather than clamped away, so freeing space (collapsing the resource meters) returns it. The **Resources** dock at the foot is collapsible for the same reason, keeping its headline numbers in the header when closed.
  - The panel belongs to the **app**, not to any one window, so opening a file from the explorer doesn't take the explorer away with it; it follows the active chat and says so when there isn't one. Toggled from the workspaces sidebar or Ctrl/⌘+\. A workspace can also carry its own **file explorer as a window** (the `files` kind), rooted at a project folder and as many as the work needs, for when the folder belongs beside the work rather than on the far side of the screen.
  - Prefs persist per session and actually shape the prompt. **Both readouts move as you type**: the composer's context gauge and this panel's total, bar, and breakdown all count the unsent draft as a **Your message** source, so the cost of what you're about to send is visible before you send it rather than only after the reply lands.
- **Spec-driven development** `[shipped]`, first-class Kiro-style spec workflow built from the conversation. Pick a **methodology** (per-chat + global default): `spec-tasks` (**Spec → Tasks**, spec.md → tasks.md with no separate plan), `nekko-agent` (**Spec → Plan → Tasks**, spec.md → plan.md → tasks.md), `kiro` (requirements.md → design.md → tasks.md), or `lean` (single spec.md). Spec → Tasks is the default, while the other methodologies remain available. Each artifact has its own **Build/Update** button and is **chained** where the methodology provides earlier artifacts as context. When a chat has multiple context folders, the Spec panel **follows the primary folder** chosen in the Folders section right above it, and its own project select overrides that until the choice above moves again, so spec and tasks can be viewed, built, and toggled per workspace without ever showing one project's docs while the pane says another is primary. A "Build all from chat" button runs the whole pipeline. The **tasks artifact renders as an interactive checklist** whose toggles write `[ ]`/`[x]` back to the file, with a progress bar. Lives in the Context Inspector's Spec panel. A **Live** toggle keeps the primary spec rebuilt after every reply.
- **Guardrails** `[shipped]`, risky-command classifier (destructive fs, force push, curl|sh, registry, privilege escalation) + allow/ask/deny policy engine with a default ruleset; settings UI with a guardrails JSON editor. Each rule's **default** action is marked with a small dot on its allow/ask/deny control, and a **Reset to defaults** button at the bottom of the section (with a "N rules differ from the defaults" count) restores the shipped ruleset, dropping any custom rules.
- **Workspace jail** `[shipped; links resolved 2026-10-15]`, the default sandbox mode (Settings → Sandbox) confines the file tools (`read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `list_dir`, `glob`, `grep`) to the chat's added folders; a path outside every folder is refused with a message that names the folder to add or the mode to switch. Since 2026-10-15 the check follows links as well as strings (#550): after the normalized-path comparison, the deepest existing ancestor of the target and of each workspace folder is resolved on disk (symlinks on Unix, symlinks and junctions on Windows), so a link inside a workspace that points outside it is refused even though its path reads as inside, while a link to another added folder stays allowed. A file that does not exist yet is judged by where its nearest existing parent really is. The TS host and the engine daemon (`crates/nekko-tools`) apply the same rule, held together by the tools golden set, which creates such a link at test time on each platform. `bash` and background processes remain unconfined by this mode, as before.
- **Memory** `[shipped]`, markdown memory store (global + per-workspace) with CRUD and a manager UI.

### Workspaces, indexing & connectors

- **Workspaces** `[shipped]`, multi-root folder add/remove/activate, chats bound to one primary workspace plus optional supporting workspaces so index + per-project memory engage across every selected folder while tools keep the primary as their working directory.
- **Indexer** `[shipped]`, workspace file tree, regex-based code outline, search; index-grounded context merged across a chat's selected primary and supporting folders.
- **Projects page** `[removed 2026-07-18]`, the dedicated Projects view (folder add/remove, index status, file browser) was retired to keep the rail focused. Folders are added from a chat's **+ → Folder** menu or the Context Inspector; files are browsed in the right panel's Folders accordions; the index keeps powering `@` file mentions and context under the hood.
- **Connectors** `[shipped]`, GitHub/Linear/Slack/Discord (token-based, real REST), Jira (site URL + email + API token) and Microsoft Teams (incoming webhook for posting, optional Graph token for reads) `[shipped]`, Gmail/Drive (paste an OAuth access token; one-click OAuth flow still pending Google client creds); connector snippets fed into context. **Official brand icons** (Simple Icons, CC0, in brand colours) and **validate-on-connect**, Connect stores the token and immediately does a real fetch, surfacing a bad token as an inline error instead of silently "connecting"; each card links to where to get its token.
- **Nekko inside other agent tools** `[shipped]`, one-click install of the app as an MCP subagent into detected agent CLIs (Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Windsurf): the app is already an MCP server (`nekko-agent mcp`), so connecting writes that server into the other tool's config (a backup is made first), and a copyable config snippet covers tools we can't write to. From then on Claude Code or Codex can hand Nekko scoped tasks, which run under this app's guardrails on this machine's models.

### Tooling & integrations

- **MCP client** `[shipped]`, connect stdio MCP servers **or streamable-HTTP endpoints** (a URL + optional bearer token, added via "+ Add URL" in Settings); their tools merge into the agent loop namespaced `mcp__<id>__<tool>`, with per-chat toggles and live status. Under the engine daemon a chat turn syncs and calls its MCP servers in the daemon, approvals included, so an MCP call never waits on the TS host `[implemented 2026-10-12, PF14]`. Sub-agents (`spawn_agent`) are driven by the daemon too: the TS host checks the delegation and creates and admits the child session, and the daemon runs the child's turn, stops it with the parent, resumes it once after a transient failure and returns its answer `[implemented 2026-10-12, PF14]`. Verified against `@modelcontextprotocol/server-filesystem` (stdio) and a live Hypergate gateway (HTTP). An **MCP menu sits in the composer's instrument strip, just right of Tools**: it lists every configured server with a live status dot (connected / offline / checking), its name and transport, and an **enable/disable switch** (server enablement is global, so turning one on offers its tools to every chat); it probes live status on open, and offers **Add MCP server** (jumps to Settings) plus a recommendation to manage servers securely with the **Hypergate** app.
- **Hypergate integration** `[shipped]`, Nekko Agent pairs with [Hypergate](https://hypergate.app), the Nekko Labs MCP server runtime and manager, in one click from either side, and then runs it as a tab in this window.
  - **Detect.** Nekko Agent probes the local `hypergated` daemon at startup (in the engine daemon when one runs, otherwise in the TS host, so it works in every edition) and shows a card in Settings → MCP servers naming its version, its managed-server count, and its port. With no daemon running, a quiet **Get Hypergate** hint makes the companion discoverable without nagging.
  - **Connect (from Nekko Agent).** One click claims this install's own scoped agent token from the daemon, saves the aggregated gateway as a single MCP entry (every server Hypergate manages, one URL), brings its tools online in every chat, and opens the Hypergate tab. Re-connecting replaces that entry rather than adding a second.
  - **Connect (from Hypergate).** Hypergate's **Connect Nekko Agent** button opens `nekko-agent://hypergate/connect?port=N`, which brings Nekko Agent forward (launching it if it was closed) and runs the same flow. The link names a port and carries no credential: Nekko Agent reads the daemon's identity back over loopback and asks the user before connecting, because any local program can open a URL and a gateway's tools reach every chat.
  - **Scoped, not borrowed.** The token is Hypergate's own per-agent credential rather than the master gateway token, so the pairing is visible and governable from both sides: Hypergate's Agents list shows Nekko Agent online, with its own per-server permissions, usage history, and revocation.
  - **Hypergate as a window.** The manager's UI runs in a workspace window (`hypergate` pane kind) with the daemon's live version, server count, and connected agent in its header, so adding or auditing MCP servers never means leaving Nekko Agent. It says so and offers to reconnect when the daemon stops.
- **`nekko-agent` CLI + MCP server** `[shipped]`, run the host in-process or against a running server over HTTP+WS; `nekko-agent status|sessions|chat|mcp|watch`; `nekko-agent mcp` exposes Nekko Agent as an MCP *server* (chat/list/new/get/status) so other agents can trigger agents + swarms. Bundled into the npx package.
- **Installed means set up** `[shipped 2026-09-28]`, installing the desktop app is enough for the CLI and MCP to work, with nothing to export or `npm install`. The **local API server is on by default** (loopback only, a token generated on first run). The app **ships the CLI inside itself** (one CommonJS file run by the app's own Electron, so no Node or npm is involved) and links an `nekko-agent` launcher onto PATH on first run (the user PATH on Windows, a marked shell-profile line elsewhere). The address and token go into a **link file** the CLI reads when no flag or env var says otherwise, stamped with the app's process id, so a terminal falls back to the local data directory the moment the app quits or crashes instead of dialling a dead port. **Onboarding pre-checks** every detected agent tool and adds Nekko Agent as an MCP server to the checked ones on Next (skip or uncheck to leave a config alone). Entries the app wrote are **kept current** when the port, address or token changes; entries written by hand are never touched. The **Server tab** owns the address, token and environment in one block: where it listens (this computer, the network, or a specific interface), the port, the **address clients are told to use** when that differs (a tunnel, a container port, a Tailscale name), the token, and copyable env lines for bash or PowerShell, masked on screen. The CLI tab shows whether the command is installed and on PATH, with Install/Reinstall, and the npm package stays the fallback for the web and self-hosted editions.

### Mascot, polish & onboarding

- **Aphelion mascot** `[shipped]`, a 2D hand-drawn **outline astronaut cat**: every pose is a single ink stroke (`var(--ink)`) on the theme's paper (`var(--paper)`), no color fills except the ginger tail and inner ears, so she rides the theme tokens and works on light and dark chrome without per-theme tuning. A turbulence **line boil** re-seeds a few times a second so the strokes wobble like frame-by-frame ink even when the pose is still; it switches off under reduced motion and while the document is hidden. The body and helmet carry a paper fill purely for occlusion (nearer shapes hide the lines behind them, the inked-animation trick). The activity poses are unchanged: lying (with tucked front-paw rolls peeking under the chest), waking stand, stretching, bug watch, sleeping. `AphelionAvatar` (the Agent tab, the send control, login heroes) and `MiniAphelion` (inline spinner, heavier stroke for 18px legibility) share the outline dome; the boil stays exclusive to the rail mascot so the composer stays calm. (The filled astronaut illustration this replaced is described in TASKS T114/T124.)
- **Onboarding wizard** `[planned]`, the first-run setup described under "First-run setup": a full-screen, every-step-skippable wizard covering theme, providers, and integrations, replayable from Settings, identical on every edition because it shares the renderer.
- **Theme presets** `[planned]`, System / Light / Dark plus a set of elegant tech palettes (e.g. Nebula, Terminal, Nord, Solar, Ember), each rendered as a gradient swatch circle of its colors and previewed live on click. Presets extend the existing token theming (mode + accent + a second accent, plus deeper surface overrides where a palette needs them) and appear in Settings → Appearance too.
- **Polish** `[shipped]`, welcome/empty states, command palette (Ctrl+K), keyboard shortcuts, toasts, onboarding banner, persistent Context Inspector prefs, app icon, boot smoke test. Shortcuts are **discoverable, not folklore**: new agent (Ctrl/⌘+N) and new terminal (Ctrl/⌘+`) show their key hint next to the matching item in the workspaces **+** menu, on the empty-state buttons, and in the command palette. Every chord and its printed label come from one definition, so they can't drift apart, and the labels render as ⌘ on macOS.
- **Refined design system** `[shipped]`, a Warp/Linear-grade visual pass: cleaner cooler neutrals with more breathing room, soft elevation shadows on cards, a **secondary color** (`--accent-2`) paired with the accent into a **brand gradient** (the Command Center wordmark, primary buttons, the rail logo, and the active-nav indicator), focus rings, and tighter heading tracking. All theme tokens (incl. `--accent-soft`) track the user's chosen accent. `[updated 2026-10-01]` Native select popups follow the theme too: every dropdown's option list paints in the surface/ink tokens rather than white-on-white, which the rule previously missed on any select not wearing the `input` class. Approachable for non-technical users while keeping the Aphelion mascot.
- **Border beam** `[shipped]`, the border-beam motion pattern in brand colors: while the agent is working, a violet→cyan beam laps the chat composer's **border only** (the earlier interior color wash was removed, so the surface stays still as you type); the marketing site's hero window mock and download button carry the same beam (the button's speeds up on hover). Static ring without `@property` support; still under reduced motion.
- **App icon: the cat mark** `[shipped]` `[redrawn 2026-10-04]`, an **outline cat head** (two ears, a soft curve of forehead between them, a round jaw, and nothing inside the face) drawn in one **thin stroke with sharp corners** (miter joins, flat caps) on a deep squircle tile with a hairline rim light: simple enough to survive 16 pixels, and the same mark the rail carries beside the wordmark and the desktop title bar carries on its own. Philip chose it from a proof sheet of jaw depths, ear shapes, line weights and corner styles ("D7, thin, sharp"); the first cut of the day had eyes and a smile and round joins, which read soft. One vector source (`apps/desktop/scripts/icon-art.cjs`, mirrored in `renderer/components/BrandMark.tsx` for the in-app mark) is rasterized by Electron itself (`npx electron apps/desktop/scripts/make-icons.cjs`), which writes `build/icon.svg`, `icon.png` (512), a true multi-size `icon.ico` (16/24/32/48/64/128/256), the web/PWA `icon-512.png` + `icon.svg`, the two NSIS installer bitmaps, and the phone app's icons (`apps/mobile/assets/images`: the tile as `icon.png`/`favicon.png`, bare layers for Android's adaptive icon and the splash); `node scripts/make-site-icon.cjs` writes the marketing site's `favicon.svg`, which the site also uses as its nav and footer brand mark. Small sizes are drawn with a heavier stroke instead of being downscaled from the large one. The mascot (the full-body agent cat) is unchanged and still fronts the site's hero and social image. This retires the 2026-09 orbit mark (a comet on a tilted ring) and, before it, the pre-rebrand orange pixel cat.
- **Fresh color palette** `[shipped]`, the default accent is a modern **indigo-violet** (`#6d5efc`) paired with a **cyan** secondary into a violet→cyan brand gradient, the current developer-tool look (Linear/Vercel-era), replacing the original salmon/orange. Neutrals were cooled to match. Existing installs are **migrated** off the old default automatically (users who picked their own accent keep it); the accent is still fully recolorable in Settings. Applied across the app, the marketing website, and the branded installer art. The Aphelion mascot stays a ginger cat, and lives in the UI rather than in the app icon (see the orbit mark).
- **One title bar, no menu bar** `[shipped]`, the desktop window used to stack three strips above the app: an OS title bar, a File/Edit/View/Window menu, and then the UI. The menu is gone (it only duplicated shortcuts the app already owns, and macOS keeps its own, which lives in the system bar rather than the window), and the title bar is now the app's: a 38px strip in `--paper` carrying the Nekko Agent wordmark, with the **native window buttons drawn into it** — Window Controls Overlay on Windows and Linux, painted in the app's own background and repainted whenever the theme changes, and macOS traffic lights inset onto the same line. The strip is the drag region, so dragging it moves the window and double-clicking maximises. The wordmark moved out of the rail, which only ever showed it on hover, so the rail starts on its first destination and the app is a row shorter overall. Reload (Ctrl/⌘+R) and devtools (F12) were the only menu items worth keeping and are bound directly. Nothing renders in the web or phone builds, which have chrome of their own. `[updated 2026-10-11]` The strip drops the "Nekko Agent" wordmark: it leads with the cat mark alone at 22px (was 16px beside the wordmark; still named "Nekko Agent" for screen readers), followed by the current view's heading at 17px (was 14px, so it now clearly outranks the 11.5px version pill) and a slimmer version pill (20px tall, was 24px). Verified in renderer captures emulating the desktop chrome (Windows light/dark, macOS traffic-light inset); the packaged app's native caption buttons were not captured. Same day, second pass: the heading is 24px, and every view's page title (Settings, Skills, Workflows, Memory, Design, Connectors, Model Providers, Nekko Server, Training) shares that size through one `view-title` style so switching tabs never jumps; the version pill drops to 10.5px text and 18px tall; and the cat mark sits 4px below centre so it lines up with the heading's letters rather than its line box. Third pass: the mark is 28px (25% larger) and, off macOS, centred straight above the rail's first icon. Fourth pass: the title bar names every view, not just Agents (the rail labels, so it follows the app language), and in the desktop shell each view drops its own page title so the name appears once; sub-page headings such as Marketplace and New training run stay. The web and phone builds keep their page titles. `[updated 2026-10-12]` The strip is 58px tall (was 38px), giving the cat mark and heading 10px more space above them. The native caption buttons (Window Controls Overlay, `TITLEBAR_HEIGHT`) keep their original 38px size, and on Windows/Linux the trailing title-bar controls (Agents toolbar, developer server controls) sit in that 38px band, with every toolbar label (Focus, Dynamic, Grid, All, Agents, Terminals, Panels) and icon centred on one line with the minimise glyph, measured on the letters' cap-height midline: 21.5px from the top (19px, the overlay's centre, plus 2.5px requested after checking against the real caption buttons). Verified by hidden renderer measurement of the real toolbar; the match with the native glyph was judged by the user in the running app, not captured; native caption buttons and macOS were not captured.
- **Multi-language UI (i18n)** `[shipped]`, system-language detection + English fallback; en/es/fr/de/pt/ja/zh.
- **Data & privacy controls** `[shipped]`, delete chats by window, Reset configs, Delete everything; settings export/import.

### Distribution & platforms

- **Supported platforms** `[decided 2026-10-13]`: Windows (x64, arm64), Linux (x64, arm64) and macOS on Apple silicon. **Intel Macs are not supported** (Philip, 2026-10-13: add them back when there is demand). The engine daemon cannot be built for them (ONNX Runtime has no prebuilt x86_64 macOS library) and the desktop app already shipped arm64 only, so on an Intel Mac the web edition, `npx nekko-agent` and the CLI's local mode refuse to start with a message saying so; the CLI can still drive Nekko Agent running on another machine (`--url`). The model catalog offers no Intel Mac llama.cpp build.
- **Marketing website** `[shipped]`, static site (nekkoagent.com) rebuilt in an immersive deep-space style inspired by liquidink.design's GitHub concept: drifting starfield canvas, nebula gradients, an **Nekko Agent** display hero (leading with what it does for you, "AI help on your computer", rather than with the technology, see AN10b), glass panels, numbered chapters with scroll reveals, the astronaut Aphelion drifting in zero-g with a spark lapping it in the hero, plus the app-window mock, positioning cards, and edition cards. Dark-only by design; reduced-motion safe; still no-framework hand-crafted HTML/CSS/JS. The narrative leads with the **local-models spotlight** (chapter 01), then **"The harness is the other half of the model"** (chapter 02): the argument that a model you run yourself competes on real work through the scaffolding around it, laid out as a three-group spec-sheet manifest (what it sees / what it can reach / when it's wrong) rather than a card grid. The download chapter is a **smart download button**: it detects the visitor's OS and architecture, resolves the real asset URL from the GitHub Releases API (with the releases page as the offline/rate-limited fallback), and a caret opens a menu of every other platform and format (installer, portable, distro package), keyboard navigable and marking the build we picked for you. The **hero CTA is that same button**, so the first click downloads instead of scrolling to a second button, and the GitHub link beside it is a quiet outline pill. Both share **one button shape with hypergate.app**, the sibling product: a fully rounded single-line pill, dark ink on the brand gradient, the version in a mono chip behind a hairline divider, and an equal-height outline pill next to it (format and architecture move to the link's tooltip and the caption under the button). Below the download button, terminal people get the **same build as a one-liner in their own shell's syntax** (PowerShell `iwr`, `curl` + `open` on macOS, `curl` + `chmod` on Linux) with a copy button; it appears only once the real asset URL resolves, so the command is never a guess.
- **Documentation hub** `[implemented 2026-10-12]`, nekkoagent.com/docs, laid out like svelte.dev/docs: the page list on the left grouped into Introduction, Using Nekko Agent, Contributing and For AI agents; the article in the middle; "On this page" on the right with the section being read highlighted; previous/next links and an "Edit this page on GitHub" link at the foot; Ctrl/Cmd+K (or `/`) search over every section; copy buttons on code blocks; and a drawer for the page list on phones. `/docs` opens on **Getting started**: who we are and what we care about, a few bullets on what the app does, then installing it (the installer, or from source: the Git, Node 22.12+ and Rust prerequisites with each platform's C/C++ toolchain, clone, `npm install`, `npm run dev`). Every page renders from one Markdown file in the repo (`docs/hub/nav.json` maps them; the existing guides in `docs/` are reused, not copied), so a doc is edited once. For agents, each page is also served as Markdown at `/docs/<page>.md`, `/llms-full.txt` holds every page in one file, and `llms.txt` and `sitemap.xml` list the pages. The site still has no build step on Vercel: `npm run docs` (`scripts/build-docs.mjs`) writes the pages into `apps/website/docs`. The **Docs site** workflow (`.github/workflows/docs-site.yml`) keeps them current without anyone remembering to: on a pull request that touches a docs source it rebuilds the pages and, only if the output changed, commits them to the PR branch with the repository App token (so CI re-runs on that commit); on main (push, a six-hourly schedule, or dispatch) it opens a `docs-site/regenerate` PR instead, since main only changes through PRs. A fork PR cannot be pushed to, so there it fails and asks for `npm run docs`. `npm test` checks that every docs link and heading anchor resolves and that the workflow watches every page source. A PR that changes user-facing behaviour updates the page describing it (AGENTS.md). Dark-only, matching the marketing site. The homepage nav and footer link to it.
- **Desktop packaging & release** `[shipped]`, electron-builder (win/mac/linux), GitHub Actions release workflow, branded NSIS installer + Windows uninstaller, **macOS Developer ID signing + Apple notarization** (hardened runtime, entitlements, stapled ticket, and a `codesign`/`stapler`/`spctl` check that fails the release rather than the user's machine; ad-hoc signing survives only as the fallback that keeps unsigned local builds launchable), and `electron-updater` against published GitHub releases. The desktop title bar's version pill is the complete update experience: it becomes **Check for updates** on hover or keyboard focus, keeps fast checks legible for at least 500 ms, confirms `latest`, offers **Download & install**, **Download only**, or **Skip** for a newer version, shows real download progress, stages **Install & restart**, stays visible while restarting, reports **Updated to vX.Y.Z** on the next launch, and exposes retry/details on failure. Skipping suppresses only that release and a manual check restores it. Settings uses the same live state and actions, while the existing first-run auto-check consent remains in place. Web keeps the same-build comparison and refresh fallback instead of desktop-only install controls. `[updated 2026-10-02]` Settings → Updates is now **per-check**, one toggle each: **App updates** (the version check above), **Provider model lists** (a background re-read of every configured provider's catalog, on by default, which is how a new ChatGPT model shows up in the picker without an app release), and **Skills catalog** (a background refresh of the skills marketplace shelf, opt-in). A **Refresh catalogs now** button runs the metadata checks on demand; they only swap lists, nothing is downloaded or installed. Released through v0.6.0, which is the first build actually signed with the Nekko Labs Developer ID certificate and notarized by Apple.
- **Self-hosted web edition** `[shipped]`, `npm run web` / `npx nekko-agent` serves the same UI; localhost bind by default, token to expose.
- **Docker edition** `[shipped]`, multi-stage image, compose with volume workspaces + `host.docker.internal`, non-root, GHCR publish.
- **Native Android app** `[implemented 2026-10-11; device verification pending]`, Kotlin / Jetpack Compose in `apps/android`: pairing, the computer's chats, live streamed replies, queue/stop, approvals and questions over the E2E relay. Phone-side models, push and Play distribution are planned. See "Native Android app (2026-10-11)".
- **Native phone apps** `[in build 2026-10-03; superseded as the target by the 2026-10-07 native decision, kept as the reference]`, a purpose-built Expo (React Native) app for iOS and Android that drives the user's computer over the relay and runs models on the phone (see "Your agent in your pocket"). Replaces the earlier Capacitor shell, which wrapped the desktop renderer (shipped 2026-06, never published to the stores; the full app on a phone remains available through a phone browser over the relay). CI builds an Android APK and an iOS simulator build. Store listings, signing and real-device push still need the developer accounts.
- **Remote push (relay-mediated)** `[shipped]`, APNs + FCM, dependency-free, content-free "a run finished" notifications, tokens registered **per device** and dropped on revocation; real delivery pending creds + device.
- **Phone remote control** `[shipped]`, run and manage chats, training, and goals from a paired phone. Relay v2 (per-device connections, unicast routing, rate/size limits), agent-side **device registry** with 10-minute single-use pairing codes, rename/revoke (live kick), and secret rotation; Remote access settings card (device list, expiring pair QR); phone **bottom tab bar**; remote access persists across restarts. Managed relay live at `wss://nekko-agent-relay.fly.dev` (free during beta; entitlement gate built for the Cloud launch); self-hosting is one Docker command (`ghcr.io/nekko-labs/nekko-agent-relay`, Compose/Coolify/Fly documented). Verified end to end through the live managed relay (enrollment, single-use codes, remote device management, revocation). See [docs/REMOTE.md](docs/REMOTE.md).
- **Messaging channels** `[shipped 2026-09-30]`, drive a chat from a messaging app, Telegram first. A long-poll bridge in the host binds each allowlisted chat id to one session, streams replies into one self-editing message, and turns approvals and `ask_user` questions into inline buttons; off by default and default-deny, configured on the Connectors page. Slack and WhatsApp adapters follow the same seam later.

### Nekko Agent

## Build storage maintenance

Implemented maintenance command (Windows cleanup validation in progress): `npm run clean:builds` previews reproducible outputs across registered worktrees and known shared Rust pools; `-- --apply` explicitly deletes eligible outputs older than 14 days by default. `--older-than=0` permits an immediate approved cleanup. `--global` includes known download caches, not model downloads, user app data, toolchain installations, source, branches or evidence. Tracked files, junction targets and primary development app dependencies/runtime/output/dev-daemon are protected. Git ownership/access failures skip the affected checkout rather than changing trust settings. Windows checks active build/install processes and runtime executable paths; Unix apply requires explicit idle confirmation because automatic ownership detection is not implemented. Extra active paths can be protected with `--protect=<path>`. No automatic worktree removal or background deletion. Transient capture/performance CI artifacts now expire after 14 days; durable PR evidence follows the existing publication policy.
 Cloud (paid, hosted)

- **Cloud foundation** `[shipped]`, multi-account hosted edition wrapping the same engine + renderer per account; email+password auth, bearer tokens, per-account data isolation, entitlements (free/pro/team) gated server-side. The OSS app never license-checks.
- **Phone↔local-model relay** `[shipped]`, see "Phone remote control" above: relay v2 + device registry + revocation + managed instance on Fly.io. The relay carries an **entitlement gate** (`NEKKO_RELAY_AUTHZ_URL` → the cloud's `/api/relay/authorize`, which checks the account and returns the plan's device allowance) so the managed relay becomes a paid Cloud perk at launch while self-hosting stays free; the gate is off during beta.
- **Payments (Stripe)** `[shipped]`, Checkout (upgrade to Pro/Team) + Customer Portal (manage/cancel) + signature-verified webhooks that drive an account's plan (and therefore its entitlements). Hand-rolled against the Stripe REST API (no SDK dependency) and gated on `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, so the cloud server runs fully without a Stripe account; real charges light up once keys are configured.
- **ZDR + cloud history/files** `[planned]`, retention modes, encrypted-at-rest sync engine, ZDR badges + audit log.
- **Managed connectors** `[planned]`, pre-registered OAuth apps for Gmail/Drive/Slack/Discord.
- **Relay tiers + free push + fair-share limiter** `[planned]`, see "Hosted relay tiers, free notifications and what the server may know" under phone remote control: free content-free push for all, a limited and fair-queued free traffic tier, Cloud lifts the limits, self-hosting gets everything for free.
- **PII-free server analytics** `[planned]`, aggregate counters only (platforms, versions, coarse region, content-free event names, tier and limit hits), daily-salted identifiers, 24-hour raw-log rotation, a visible opt-out on both apps.

## Scope Boundaries

- **No native node modules**: keeps Electron rebuild pain away; ripgrep/git are spawned via child_process with JS fallbacks. Native speed comes from the separate Rust engine daemon (`nekkod`), a standalone binary shipped beside the app, never a module loaded into Node or Electron. (Technical constraint; see `TASKS.md`.)
- **Not a full IDE**: the file editor is a lightweight mono textarea (+ rendered markdown), not Monaco/CodeMirror; no language servers, debugger, multi-cursor, or refactoring tooling. The browser pane is a preview/utility surface, not a hardened general web browser. The Design board (planned) shows snapshots, not an editable vector canvas.
- **Cloud never runs your shell/filesystem tools on our servers**: inference and tool calls always execute on *your* paired machine. The cloud is a sync + relay + billing layer, not a remote executor.
- **The OSS app never checks a license**: paid features are gated server-side in Cloud only; the open-source editions are fully functional and free.
- **The relay is a dumb pipe**: it authenticates/routes and forwards ciphertext; it does not inspect or store message bodies.
- Pricing tiers (Free OSS / Pro / Team) are drafts pending cost modeling, see [spec-web-and-hosted.md](spec-web-and-hosted.md) §6.
- **Workflows don't execute untrusted code from strangers**: a git-triggered workflow that checks out a pull request runs that contributor's code with this machine's tool access. Local CI runner templates must only auto-fire on trusted refs (own branches, collaborators' PRs); running arbitrary fork PRs is an explicit opt-in with sandboxed (yolo-off, approval-gated) tool policy, not a default.
- **Subscription sign-in rides first-party OAuth clients**: the Claude and ChatGPT subscription flows use the public OAuth client of the vendor's own CLI. Vendor terms are written around their own apps, so the feature is offered as-is, clearly labelled, with the API-key path unaffected. If a vendor cuts it off, the app degrades to API keys, not a broken product.

## Open Questions

- Hosting/runtime for Cloud: **the relay now runs on Fly.io** (`nekko-agent-relay.fly.dev`, Nekko Labs org, single machine since rooms are per-machine memory); the Cloud app's host is still open (Fly.io is the natural default now). Relay scaling beyond one machine needs sticky routing (fly-replay by room) when it matters.
- E2E key-management UX (recovery vs zero-knowledge trade-offs).
- ~~Mobile: PWA first, or native shells later?~~ Resolved 2026-10-03: a purpose-built native app (Expo + llama.cpp), with the PWA kept for the full app in a phone browser. Revised 2026-10-07: platform-native SwiftUI (apps/ios) and Kotlin/Compose (apps/android); Expo stays as the reference until parity.
- Phone model storage: models live in the app's Documents folder, which iOS includes in device backups. Exclude them from backup (needs a native flag), or move them somewhere the OS may purge?
- Exact ZDR boundary for cloud-configured (non-local) providers without ZDR tiers, likely "not allowed in ZDR workspaces."
- Do desktop + local web share one data dir by default, or separate? (Lean: share, so the web UI sees your desktop sessions.)
- "Nekko Agent" as a local provider in onboarding now deep-links to **Nekko Server**, where the engine, the library and the borrowed folders all live. It should still become a real one-click managed-model install rather than a link to a tab.
- Microsoft Teams reads need Microsoft Graph OAuth, which requires an app registration; v1 supports posting via incoming webhook and reads via a pasted token. Whether to register a first-party Nekko Graph app is open.
- Full Gmail/Drive OAuth flow (needs Google client creds); macOS signing + notarization is **done**: the `nekko-labs` org secrets are in place and releases from v0.6.0 on are Developer ID signed and notarized (see [docs/signing.md](docs/signing.md)). Windows Authenticode is pending SignPath Foundation. The npm publish of `nekko-agent` needs an npm credential that may publish it: the v0.6.1 run failed with a 403 on the old package name, so either `NPM_TOKEN` must be able to publish the new name or the first publish is done by hand, after which a Trusted Publisher can be bound to `nekko-labs/nekko-agent`. Provisioning details in [provisioning.md](../../obsurdian/projects/nekko-agent/provisioning.md).


## Chat isolation and prompt customization

- New online, persisted chats use a dedicated Git branch/worktree for each selected repository, provisioned on first send after project selection. `[updated 2026-10-03]` The checkout's folder and branch are named in words rather than after the chat's id: `nekko/brisk-amber-otter` (an adjective, a colour and an animal, drawn at random and kept unique per repository), or a slug of the chat's title when the user or the agent has already chosen one (`nekko/fix-login-bug`, `-2` on a collision), since a new chat is usually provisioned before its title exists. Settings > Git management maps each folder back to its chat through the chat's own record of the checkout; folders named the old way still resolve. Existing chats are not moved; sub-agents share their parent's checkout. Non-Git folders remain ordinary folders. Provisioning failures stop the turn rather than falling back to a shared checkout.
- This is what lets several agent chats work on one repository at the same time: each has its own branch and folder, so their edits, builds and commits never collide with each other or with the user's own checkout.
