Terminals opened beside a chat (split from its pane) and its Agent commands log start in the chat's worktree.
- **Agent command log availability** `[implemented; desktop visual verification pending]`: the read-only Agent commands terminal contains shell/setup/background-command output, not the full agent transcript. Logs are created on first command and retained only in backend memory, so a restored chat after a backend restart may have no command snapshot. A missing/empty snapshot explains this instead of silently showing a blank terminal; a failed read shows an unavailable/retry message. First live output replaces the notice, and commands in subsequent turns reactivate the same log without dropping retained output. Snapshots remain local for `agent_` terminals even with daemon-owned PTYs; no new persistence or cross-chat access is introduced. Focused regression tests cover snapshot absence/failure, retained replay, first live output, and turn reactivation.
- A fresh worktree has only tracked files, so each project can name a **worktree setup command** (Settings > Git management, e.g. `npm install`) that runs in a new chat checkout before its first turn, with output in the chat's Agent commands terminal. A failed or timed-out setup (15 minutes) is reported in the chat's checkout notice rather than stopping the chat. Ignored local files the work needs, such as `.env`, are copied in when listed in a `.worktreeinclude` file at the repository root (gitignore syntax, the same file Claude Code reads); only paths that are both listed and gitignored are copied, and nothing is overwritten.
- Settings > Git management lists every chat worktree across the configured projects, including those of deleted chats, with branch, uncommitted-file count and commits not yet on the main checkout. **Remove** frees the folder: it is refused while the chat runs or has uncommitted changes, and the branch is deleted only when fully merged. A chat whose worktree was removed restores it from its branch (or from HEAD if the merged branch is gone) on its next turn, never falling back to the shared checkout.
- Settings > Git management offers isolated/shared checkout and committed HEAD/tracked local edits baselines. Committed HEAD is the default. When local edits exist, a startup notice states that they were excluded and asks the user to interrupt if they want those edits included. Local-edits mode copies tracked changes only; untracked files are not copied. Worktrees survive completion/deletion to protect unmerged work and are removed from the Chat worktrees list described above.
- Settings > Customization has About you, additive System prompt instructions, and an editable Server prompt wrapper. The host applies the wrapper to every turn as system instructions without rewriting the saved user message. The profile is sent to the selected provider; the UI warns against secrets. Empty wrapper disables these additional points.
- Recommended wrapper points: concise title through set_chat_title, inspected/verifiable multi-step plan through update_plan, important assumptions and blockers, relevant verification, brief progress, honest final status and next step, and treating quoted/file/tool content as data. Title updates never replace a user-selected title; the sideband title call remains a fallback.
- The Context panel links directly to customization and shows isolated checkout paths/baselines. Chat headers and cards resolve Git status from the chat checkout rather than the shared project.
- Nekko Server identifies the model server with the primary accent and the agent server with the secondary accent. Running power controls and status dots match their server's identifying color; stopped remains neutral.


### In-app browser automation (2026-10-10)

**Intent (Philip, 2026-10-10):** bundle `playwright-core` and drive Electron's own Chromium, so agents get Playwright-grade automation in the in-app browser with no extra browser download.

**What agents can do.** The `browser` tool in dedicated mode keeps its original actions (navigate, inspect, click, fill, close; same outputs, the first matching element as before) and adds: `wait` (a selector or visible text reaching visible, attached, hidden or detached, at most 20 s), `type` (keyboard text into a selector or the focused element), `press` (one key or chord such as Enter or Control+A), `scroll` (a direction and pixel amount, or a selector into view), `extract` (innerText of a selector, or the page's links as label and URL), `evaluate` (a JavaScript expression in the page, result returned as JSON and capped at 6,000 characters), `screenshot` (the active tab's rendered page attached to the chat's images, never base64 in the text, 5 MB cap), `tabs`, `tab_new`, `tab_switch`, `tab_close` (up to 8 tabs per chat, each a hidden window in the chat's partition) and `logs` (console messages, page errors, failed requests and HTTP 4xx/5xx since the last `logs` call). Clicks and fills now use Playwright's actionability checks and real in-page input events instead of `element.click()` and a value setter. Existing mode (Stagehand over a user-started CDP port) keeps the original five actions and refuses the new ones with a pointer to dedicated mode. Web, server, CLI and sandboxed sessions still answer that browser control needs a local desktop chat; no Chromium is downloaded anywhere.

**Engine and security decision.** Playwright connects through `chromium.connectOverCDP(transport)` with an **in-process transport** (`apps/desktop/src/main/agentCdpTransport.ts`), not a debugging port. The transport plays the browser-level CDP endpoint itself and reaches each agent window only through Electron's `webContents.debugger`. Rejected: launching Electron with `--remote-debugging-port` (even on 127.0.0.1 with a random port). Any local process, including a shell command the agent itself runs through `bash`, could find that port and drive every window of the app, the Nekko UI and its approval buttons included; loopback binding and port randomness do not stop a same-user process. Threat model and the boundaries that hold it:

- **The UI cannot be targeted.** The transport's only targets are the windows the bridge hands it for one chat. It answers `Target.getTargets`, `Target.attachToTarget`, `Target.attachToBrowserTarget`, `Target.setDiscoverTargets`, `Target.createBrowserContext`, `Browser.*` (except version and the download call below) and `Storage.*` at the browser level with an error, so a raw CDP session cannot be opened and other windows are never listed or attached. The Nekko UI windows' debuggers are never attached.
- **Per-chat isolation.** Each chat gets its own transport, Playwright connection, windows and in-memory partition; one chat's Playwright cannot see another's pages.
- **Command policy on page sessions.** Only page-level domains Playwright uses are forwarded (Page, Runtime, DOM, DOMSnapshot, Accessibility, CSS, Network, Fetch, Input, Emulation, Log, Overlay, IO, Inspector, Debugger, Profiler, Performance, Animation, and Target limited to auto-attach of the page's own frames and workers). Refused: `DOM.setFileInputFiles` (would read local files into the page), `Page.setDownloadBehavior`, `Target.exposeDevToolsProtocol`, `Security.*` (certificate overrides), `SystemInfo.*`, and `Page.navigate` to anything but HTTP(S) or about:blank. Playwright's `Browser.setDownloadBehavior` is acknowledged without effect: the session's `will-download` handler keeps denying downloads.
- **Who sends the actions (2026-10-12).** A turn the engine daemon drives runs the `browser` and `capture` tools in the daemon (`crates/nekko-web`): the checks, the approval prompt and the bridge request are the TS host's, held to it by a golden set, and the daemon reaches the in-app browser and the window capturer through the same private bridge, whose address and token the desktop app hands the daemon in its launch config (never its environment, so the agent's own shell commands never see the token). Existing mode, the user's own Chromium on a loopback DevTools port the user enabled, no longer goes through Stagehand and Playwright in Node: a small CDP client in the daemon attaches to the most recently active page for each action (navigate waits for load, click is a left click at the centre of the one matching element, fill clears the field and inserts the value, then the title and the first 6,000 characters of text come back), CSS selectors only, refusing a selector that matches several elements as Playwright's strict locators do, refusing a DevTools endpoint that points off this machine, and disconnecting on `close` without closing the browser. Verified against a real headless Chromium (`crates/nekko-web/tests/cdp_live.rs`).
- **Existing guardrails unchanged.** Approval before every action (the approval names the evaluate expression and discloses that screenshot pixels go to the model), HTTP(S) only, popups denied, dialogs disabled, permissions denied, sandboxed renderer with context isolation and no Node, hidden non-focusable windows, idle reaping after 10 minutes, the private token-authenticated loopback bridge as the only entry point (it carries no CDP), and the window's debugger detached before the window is destroyed (destroying an attached window stalls Electron's main thread).
- **Residual risk.** `evaluate` runs arbitrary script in the page, so "read-only" is guidance to the model, not enforcement; the page is a sandboxed web renderer with no app access, and the approval shows the expression. A page can still see what the agent types into it.

**Packaging.** `playwright-core` (pinned, no install script, no browsers) is a production dependency of the desktop app, loaded on first use with `require` and kept external to the main bundle so electron-builder packs it; `bundleExternals.test.ts` guards both and that no `playwright` or `@playwright/test` package (which download browsers) slips in. Playwright's artifacts folder is fixed under the app's userData `agent-browser` folder instead of a temp folder per chat.

**Status.** Implemented. Verified by unit tests with fakes (transport policy and routing, page actions, bridge tabs/screenshot/cleanup, host validation and approval text) and by a real Electron smoke run (`apps/desktop/scripts/browser-automation-smoke.mjs`: every action against a local fixture, file:// navigation blocked, no debugging switch or DevToolsActivePort file, a UI stand-in window never attached, a raw transport exposes no other pages and refuses a browser CDP session, windows stay hidden and unfocused, foreground unchanged, all windows gone after close). playwright-core was also loaded from inside an asar archive under Electron and drove a hidden page. Screenshots go through Playwright (a fresh frame): the smoke run showed `capturePage` on a hidden window returning a stale frame after scrolling, which the `capture` tool's owned-window screenshot still uses. Not verified: a full electron-builder installer build, macOS and Linux, and live model use of the new actions.

### Browser approvals and token-speed accuracy

Dedicated browser tool sessions open an isolated, sandboxed Chromium window owned by Nekko, not Edge or another external browser application, driven by Playwright through an in-process CDP transport (no debugging port; see *In-app browser automation*). Every action still needs explicit approval. Existing mode remains an explicit attachment to a user-enabled local CDP browser. Browser tool windows deny popups, downloads and device permissions and use ephemeral storage separate from the app and personal browser.

Token speed is shown only for turns with usable timing for every nonempty output step. Sub-100 ms buffered samples and missing timings hide the rate rather than pairing untimed tokens with other steps timings. Approval/tool waits do not enter decode time. Starting a new turn clears the previous speed.

### App control and isolated sessions (implemented subset; planned and unverified boundaries)

Normal is the default execution environment. The composer Mode menu has two independent columns: permission policy (Ask, Guardrails marked Recommended, YOLO) and environment (Normal, Sandboxed). Normal sessions may use already connected capabilities within their permissions; requesting a new app or connection prompts the user before it is connected. Settings groups app-control grants under Permissions, off until enabled and scoped to explicitly selected apps. Nekko Agent itself can be granted control, without an exemption for permission changes or other sensitive actions. Prefer semantic/accessibility control and the built-in browser over blind desktop coordinates. Revocation and Stop prevent subsequent actions.

Implemented subset: selecting Sandboxed opens consent-based setup for an already installed, running Linux Docker engine and an explicitly digest-pinned local image containing Python 3 and /bin/sh. Setup does not install a runtime, start its service or pull an image. Missing prerequisites fail closed with no host fallback. Managed installation and Podman, OrbStack and Apple container support remain planned, not available.


Indirect sandbox boundary: session-scoped host terminals, background processes, file explorer access, spec reads/writes and MCP calls are unsupported and denied before host side effects. Unscoped user file/terminal APIs remain host-only APIs, not sandbox capabilities. Direct daemon native loops reject sandbox declarations and persisted sandbox sessions. Daemon session creation and execution-policy patches route to the owning host so persisted new-chat defaults and setup preconditions are authoritative. Setup itself transitions an empty idle chat into sandbox mode; legacy gitIsolation patches cannot downgrade it. Session-scoped image generation is denied. Sandbox send context excludes host memory. Malformed or missing scoped daemon session lookups fail closed. Apply-back remains disabled at both the router and low-level executor because concurrent external host writes cannot be excluded; baseline hashes and advisory locks are not proof of exclusive ownership. A safe ownership/transaction design is required before enabling it. Regression tests verify denied replacements, additions and deletions preserve external host edits. Tool cancellation now owns its signal before readiness checks, rejects overlapping tool calls and passes AbortSignal to the Docker CLI; container kill remains best-effort and full host cancellation/recovery acceptance is unverified. Container file and bash tools remain the scoped execution path. Focused guard tests cover file/process denial; full caller propagation, container integration and Rust verification remain subject to the checks recorded with implementation.

A new sandbox begins in Ask. The implemented executor supports only seven scoped file/shell tools: read_file, write_file, edit_file, glob, grep, list_dir and bash. MCP, connectors, network, browser and delegation are denied, not available grants. Prompt for each required capability and action; users may choose Guardrails with sandbox settings or YOLO, but neither policy widens the isolation boundary or grants host access. Selected provider requests remain host-side; provider credentials are not injected into the container. Narrow connector brokers are planned, not implemented. Sandbox sessions cannot control host apps, including the host Nekko Agent window. A browser used in a sandbox must live inside its isolated environment. Since 2026-10-12 the engine daemon owns the containers (`crates/nekko-sandbox`, `crates/nekkod/src/sandbox.rs`): setup, status, the diff, cleanup and every tool call run there with the same isolation checks, and a Stop during a command stops the container itself. The reply still runs in the app, which asks before each call and sends it to the daemon.

Explicitly provided folders are copied into the isolated session, not mounted read-write. Work stays in the copy. Diff inspection exists at the executor boundary, but safe apply-back is planned and disabled: detecting baseline changes alone cannot exclude concurrent external writes. A verified ownership/transaction design is required before host writes can be enabled. All tool paths, MCP processes, delegated sessions, file context, terminal commands, previews and connector retrieval must honor the same session capabilities. No host filesystem, Docker socket, privileged container, host networking or unselected connector access is allowed. Containerization is a bounded isolation mechanism, not a promise against every kernel/runtime vulnerability.

Availability: the Normal/Sandboxed menu separates execution environment from Ask/Guardrails/YOLO permissions, persists new-chat defaults, and provides Docker setup UI. The container executor copies selected plain files into an owned volume with no host bind mounts, host networking or Docker socket; it checks container identity, limits and isolation before operations. Indirect host file/process/spec/MCP entry points fail closed for scoped sandbox sessions. Focused live renderer checks (24), real Docker checks (11) and Rust checks (9) are reported passed for this batch. Cleanup UI now requires explicit destructive consent and passes the persisted sandbox identity through both transports; idle-only cleanup verifies ownership labels and handles stopped containers, partial creation and host restart without modifying source folders. Reconnect verifies existing running resources rather than recopying; destroyed/stopped sandbox continuation requires a newly configured chat. Mocked transport-to-host-to-container-runner Stop propagation and cleared active-run state are tested, alongside readiness signal ownership and overlap denial. Real Docker cancellation/cleanup and restart acceptance, exhaustive indirect payload regressions, multi-platform behavior and actual desktop visual acceptance remain unverified. Send/indirect enumeration and ownership design limits are recorded in reports/pr398-lifecycle-audit.md. Hidden cleanup renderer interactions pass, but generated captures are not pixel-inspected because no image/video viewing tool is available. Captured wide/narrow light/dark screenshots await parent inspection. App-control adapters and broad sandbox capability brokers remain planned. This is an incomplete working sandbox, not shipped safe apply-back or a universal isolation guarantee.
## Composer Markdown editing tools (implemented; isolated interaction checks verified, visual inspection pending)

- Scope: the existing shared desktop/web composer Markdown editor only. Drafts and sent messages remain Markdown source; this does not change Command Center layout or wall styling.
- Right-click opens Markdown controls first (bold, italic, strike, inline code, link, heading, bullet/numbered/task list, quote, code block), followed by Copy, Paste, Paste as Markdown, and Paste without formatting. Applicable controls expose pressed state; Copy is disabled without selected text. Non-empty editor selections also expose the same controls in a viewport-positioned floating toolbar.
- Formatting and text paste preserve selection and use the editor's existing undo/redo history. Ordinary paste remains plain text and routes clipboard images to the existing attachment handler. Paste without formatting inserts plain clipboard text literally (including typed Markdown); Paste as Markdown converts supported rich HTML emphasis, links, headings, lists, quotes, and code, falling back to plain text.
- Privacy/security: clipboard reads/writes happen only on explicit action. Rich clipboard HTML is parsed detached, never inserted into the app; active content and remote images are discarded, and only HTTP(S)/mailto links are retained. Clipboard permission failures show a keyboard-shortcut fallback message without changing the draft. Disabled editors and IME composition do not expose editing actions.
- Acceptance: context menu controls precede clipboard actions; selection survives menu focus; formatting toggles and active state work for applicable source spans/blocks; conversion never executes or fetches clipboard content; toolbar dismisses on Escape, outside pointer, resize, scroll, or composition. Focused unit tests and isolated real-editor Electron interaction checks cover source edits, selection, menu state, copy, paste conversion, undo, and clipboard rejection. Full-app visual inspection, native OS clipboard integration, and mobile-native behavior remain unverified. Rich conversion is intentionally limited (no table/image import or rich CSS interpretation); this is not a general rich-text editor.

## Chat chrome and composer refinement (implemented; desktop verification pending)

- Chat headers show one checkout control: isolated worktree name or current Git branch/HEAD, never both. The control retains checkout options; PR chips are removed from the header while existing PR surfaces remain available.
- The plan rail's Change plan action is a slim pencil-icon control; it still prepares an editable follow-up without submitting or mutating the plan.
- Composer text grows with input/paste, including Markdown list indentation, and scrolls its caret within the editor once capped. The full composer budget is half the smaller of the viewport and available pane height; surrounding composer controls consume part of that budget. Manually sized text areas may grow to accommodate input within the same cap. Shift+Tab outside a list retains normal keyboard focus traversal.
- Completion suppresses persisted-interruption detection until the final transcript reload settles, even if no held live reply exists. Actual interrupted transcripts retain recovery actions. This is a client-side stale-snapshot safeguard, not a claim that every provider-side interruption is fixed.
- Acceptance: one checkout chip, no header PR badges, compact icon action, latest edited/pasted text visible without page scrolling, bounded composer on narrow/short panes, and no recovery notice from stale mid-turn snapshots after completion. Focused checks cover logic; desktop before/after and composer motion evidence remain pending. No credentials or external services are required for verification.
- Insights cost and token charts have independent today/1wk/1m/6m/1y/all-time ranges (UTC days; 30/180/365-day long windows), defaulting to 1m. Totals reflect the range; monthly projections remain separate. Range tests pass; desktop evidence remains pending.

## Workspace context commands and connected composer

- Right-click chat workspace cards and saved-chat rows for a read-only session identifier, Open, Mark as completed, Change model, Stop, Continue, and permanent Delete. Delete bypasses Completed and asks for confirmation.
- Implemented: agent/chat `PaneFrame` top-right controls default to Complete using the existing archive flow (Completed retains chats for 60 days). Settings > Appearance offers Complete or Delete; Delete always requires confirmation. Right-click the title bar for Complete, Delete, and non-destructive Close (only removes the window, preserving the chat). Other pane kinds retain Close. Focused handler/store tests cover these actions. Wide/narrow light/dark full-app screenshots were directly inspected; isolated fixture checks verified Complete, cancelled/confirmed Delete, and Agent-tab selection on 2026-10-06.
- Ctrl (or Command) toggles individual rows; Shift selects an inclusive range in visible sidebar order. Right-click a selected row applies commands to the selected chats. Stop skips idle chats, Continue skips running chats and uses non-destructive resume. Continue currently requires a saved specific model and an existing prompt; Auto chats ask the user to choose a model first. Failures are reported per chat.
- Composer model pickers and model-provider catalogs offer Set as default on right-click. The default is labeled and pinned first; a missing default remains visible but disabled. Model changes update mounted chat composers immediately.
- The composer uses 90% of the available pane width on desktop and full usable width on narrow screens. Questions extend from its upper center at 80% of composer width, with curved shoulders and no separate horizontal tray lines. They expand upward and retract on answer, with reduced-motion support.

### Per-chat information strip (implemented and renderer verified)

PR actions and model, effort, context, cost, and online/offline information are rendered below the chat composer in a per-session footer. Wall attention uses a slim local banner rather than an orange outer ring. The existing chat-type switch, dictation control and seasonal avatar are preserved when integrating the footer. Wide/narrow light/dark full-app screenshots and synthetic PR footer expand/tuck/dismiss behavior were directly inspected on 2026-10-06. Creation-time Image/Video selection and image/compact layout consolidation remain separate planned work; video generation is not implemented.

### Competitor follow-ups: implementation and verification
Running tool rows and collapsed activity groups show a conservative no-reported-progress duration after one minute. Updates occur at minute boundaries and hidden-pane timers pause. This is not a hang diagnosis. Renderer checks and inspected recording verify the one-minute boundary, expanded two-minute state, and reset on progress. Screen-reader verification is not claimed.
Mid-run failover has a tested, optional TS-loop hook and an Auto-pool-only selector foundation. It is not wired to production chats. Host pool verification, per-route usage accounting, exhaustion triggers, daemon support and context-budget checks remain unfinished. No automatic provider switch is enabled by this foundation.

Daemon HTTP response handling checks for malformed/non-JSON bodies and reports channel plus HTTP status without echoing raw bodies. Valid JSON API error envelopes remain supported. Focused response tests verified; the original desktop failure and upstream cause are not reproduced. No visual change.

## Experimental outbound resource queue

An off-by-default developer experiment adds a manual coordinator section to Nekko Server. Register a local resource, inspect jobs, explicitly claim one and open its prompt in a new agent chat. Explicitly submit the final reply as untrusted output, never automatic approval. See docs/resource-queue.md for security and deployment gates.

### Chat PR ownership
PR cards and session PR links track only successful PR creation calls made in that chat. Mentioned URLs, reviews, status lookups, edits and failed creations do not attach PRs. Ordinary message links remain clickable. Historical chats without creation evidence do not claim ownership.

### Composer-connected PR deck
Live PR actions appear in a centered deck emerging from the divider between the chat and footer controls. The deck reserves layout space and never overlaps those controls. Open or draft PRs expand the deck automatically; when all tracked PRs are merged or closed, a collapsed count and resolved-status summary remains without action buttons. Manual expansion lasts only while the tracked PR statuses remain unchanged. Dismissed cards retract before removal. Reduced-motion users get effectively instant transitions. Transcript creation/merge milestones remain separate. Implemented in a draft change. Desktop typecheck and 15 focused tests pass. Isolated synthetic fixture geometry and open/merged/reopened checks pass at 1200 and 700 px in light and dark themes. Matching screenshots and recordings are captured, but screenshot pixels and video motion have not been inspected; visual acceptance remains pending. Real host/provider integration, native window chrome, and other platform behavior are unverified.

## Rich responses, reports, and design workspace

### User intent
Any configured capable model should be able to create rich, portable deliverables within Nekko: photos and captured evidence, readable Markdown reports, architecture diagrams, and interactive design prototypes. This is provider-neutral, not tied to an advertised or assumed model name. Chat stays concise while a substantial research or explanation task may produce a durable linked report.

### Initial implementation (in review; not yet visually verified)
- Markdown links to local artifacts open the in-app file pane. Chat references resolve from the session's primary workspace or isolated worktree; absolute paths disambiguate multi-root chats.
- Local PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP image references offer an explicit Load image action, then display inline. Opening images in the file pane also previews them. Raster reads validate signatures and cap preview size at 8 MB. Remote images are not fetched automatically.
- Markdown files retain document/source views. HTML and SVG files gain isolated preview/source views; Mermaid fences and .mmd/.mermaid files gain diagrams with source fallback on parse errors.
- HTML preview provides Fit, phone, tablet, and desktop widths. On desktop, Open interactive preview launches a separate sandboxed window served by an ephemeral loopback HTTP transport. It has an independent response CSP, no preload/app bridge/Node access, no persistent session, and denied outbound requests, navigation, popups, downloads, and permissions. Only the authorized app main frame can request a preview (1 MB UTF-8 limit); closing the preview or owning app closes the server. The web client retains static previews and reports interactive desktop-only availability. An Electron/Chromium probe verified inline script execution, blocked external fetch, and absence of bridge, Node require, and opener; exhaustive hostile-document and cross-platform visual verification remain outstanding. An opaque sandbox origin, restrictive content policy, blocked network/form submission, and no app bridge separate generated content from the host. Prototypes should be self-contained; relative CSS/assets are not automatically bundled.
- The existing Design board remains the interactive workspace: sketch or describe, refine concepts, pin notes, open/edit source, and interact with viewport-sized previews. New safety policy also covers concept thumbnails. This is not a vector editing canvas or a proprietary design-file importer.
- Shared prompt guidance encourages reports with sources, assumptions, trade-offs, recommendations, and next steps when useful, without creating unnecessary files for simple conversations. AGENTS.md requires contemporaneous spec maintenance for feature/behavior changes.

### Acceptance and follow-up
Security tests must cover isolation, no silent local reads, raster bounds/signatures, unsafe URL handling, and broken diagram fallback. UI review must cover chat file links, inline image loading, source/preview editing, viewport controls, and actual prototype interaction. HTML artifacts can now be explicitly imported from the file pane into a chosen project board, creating an editable copy under nekko-designs/. Refinements and restores keep up to ten previous HTML snapshots; restore requires confirmation and saves the current document as a new history entry. This import/restore UI is implemented but not yet end-to-end visually verified. Native mobile parity, direct chat-to-board shortcuts, element-level visual editing, and export to external design formats remain follow-up work; this initial change must not claim them complete. Rendering an image to the user does not itself give the model vision input: multimodal tool-result delivery is a separate requirement.

### Spooky theme
The seasonal preset is displayed as Spooky in a dedicated Fun! group below the standard theme preset groups. Its persisted autumn ID remains compatible with existing settings. Seasonal merged-PR confetti is only shown while Spooky is active and disappears on theme changes. Only the bottom-left corner mascot wears the small orange wizard hat (changed 2026-10-08, reversing the earlier composer-avatar placement): the composer's send cat, the empty-wall cat and the title-bar brand mark stay undecorated, and the brand mark no longer carries a seasonal leaf. `[changed 2026-10-09]` The hat is drawn at 80% of its former size and sits higher, its brim on the crown between the ears rather than over the forehead.

### Idle energy: working animations stop costing the GPU (2026-10-09)
`[Implemented; measured in an isolated Electron benchmark on macOS (Apple Silicon); long-run Activity Monitor energy impact on a real session not yet re-measured]`

User report: on macOS, Nekko Agent's Energy Impact stayed high and GPU use sat near 66% with no local model running. Cause: while agents work, the composer beam, each working wall window's beam, the sidebar status rockets and the needs-you glow ran smooth, infinite CSS animations. Chromium re-composited them at the display's full refresh rate (120 Hz on ProMotion). A window that stays visible behind another app is not throttled, so this went on all day.

- The working beams, status rockets and needs-you glow use stepped timing: the composer beam is 48 steps a lap (15 fps), the wall beam 60 steps over its 5 s lap, and the glow and rockets 5–10 fps. Each indicator still visibly moves.
- While the window is not focused or is hidden, `<html data-app-inactive>` pauses every animation on its current frame. Working state stays drawn (beam, rocket, glow), just still, and motion resumes on focus. Implemented in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/appActivity.ts`.
- Acceptance (benchmark `apps/desktop/scripts/idle-energy-bench.cjs`, app CSS, inactive visible window, average GPU-process CPU): a busy wall (composer beam, 3 wall beams, dots, rockets, mascot) goes from 22.5% to 7.8% focused and to 0.1% unfocused. The composer beam alone goes from 14–40% to about 3%. Reduced-motion behaviour is unchanged. No new probes, requests or settings.
- Not verified: Activity Monitor's 12 h energy figure on a long real session, and Windows/Linux (the same CSS applies, but it was not measured there).

### Polish 2026-10-09: nav cat, composer focus border, dock drag landing zone
`[Implemented; verified in the isolated hidden Electron fixture 2026-10-09]`
- The nav rail's **Agents** destination icon is the pixel cat head without eyes (a silhouette that reads as an icon beside the other destinations). The corner mascot, send cat and empty-wall cat keep their eyes.
- The **composer's focus border** is softer: a 1px border at 70% accent mixed with the line colour, and a 2px (was 3px) halo outside the wall. On the Agents wall the focused composer shows the border alone; the earlier inset accent ring doubled it into a heavy 2px line.
- **Dragging a side-dock panel** lifts it out of the column, and a dashed, accent-tinted landing zone of the panel's height opens in its place (with no jump). As the pointer moves, the zone closes and reopens at the insertion point under it with a height transition, so neighbouring panels slide apart. On drop the panel is placed at the zone (before the panel below it, or last) and animates from the drop point into its slot; a cancelled drag animates back home. Reduced motion turns off the zone and landing transitions. Keyboard reordering (Alt+ArrowUp/Down) and persistence are unchanged. Acceptance: lifting opens exactly one zone; the open zone follows the pointer; the panel lands where the zone was; no zones remain after drop. Unverified: OS-native drag with a real mouse (the fixture drives synthetic DragEvents and draws its own ghost, since synthetic drags have no OS drag image).

### Calmer tinted presets and one window surface (2026-10-08)
Implemented: Nebula and Terminal keep their hue but at lower saturation. Nebula's accents are #9d8ce6/#d98bb6 (were violet-400/pink-400) and its surfaces are a near-neutral violet-grey instead of saturated indigo; Terminal's greens and surfaces are softened the same way. Anyone who already picked either preset has the old accents saved in settings; those exact saved values are read as the new ones, while any other custom color is left alone. Nebula, Terminal and Nord each define their own chat-window surface (`--win`), so windows sit in the preset's hue rather than the neutral Dark grey. On the Agents wall, the composer, the dock and the plan panel ("This prompt") share that same surface as the chat windows: the composer panel paints it once with a transparent inner card, and the plan panel is transparent over its chat window. The Add window and selected-window glows are softer. Verified by contrast tests (ink-soft on the window surface is at least 4.5:1 in every theme), preset/accent-upgrade tests and focused renderer tests; visual checks are listed in the PR.

### Visible continuation turns
Explicit typed continuation text remains a saved user message even when a request resumes an interrupted run. Newly persisted user turns emit session metadata before streaming, so a separate wall transcript sees input sent by the shared composer. Empty resume-button clicks still add no synthetic user text.

PR ownership discovery fails closed on shell provenance: only direct gh creation commands returning one bare PR URL qualify. Scripts containing creation examples, shell batches, and mixed/multiple-URL outputs do not attach PRs. This conservative rule may omit real PRs created in batches.

### Delegated interruption recovery
Implemented: after an interrupted child turn exhausts stream retries, known transient network/stream or lost-engine failures receive at most one checkpoint resume on the same child session and provider/model. Empty resume input adds no duplicate user task. Cancellation, approval, credentials, permanent request/model errors and unknown errors are not retried. Exhaustion returns the child ID, actual error and bounded saved-progress excerpt to the parent so it can continue directly or report a blocker. No route fallback or new authority is granted. Verified with scripted host tests; the layout incident was not located in checked local stores.
Planned/unverified: durable parent-child reconciliation after app restart, detection of live-but-hung tools/daemon probes, completion-aware parent supervision and end-to-end desktop recovery. Normal model completion is not proof the entire user task is finished.


### User-sent photos in PR descriptions

Implemented as shared TypeScript/Rust prompt guidance: only agent chats making or updating a PR ask about including relevant user-sent code-work photos/screenshots, and only when images exist and no scoped preference is already known. Ask before upload/publication, offering this PR only, remembered inclusion for the project or the user's projects, or exclusion. Existing explicit choices avoid repeat questions; later instructions override them. Remember choices only through authorized guidelines/preference storage and report persistence limitations. Non-PR chats do not ask or publish under this policy.

Opted-in images belong in the PR description under repository media rules, with verified published links and reference images distinguished from implementation evidence. Unrelated/sensitive images need specific permission. Required agent-captured visual evidence remains independent. Prompt regression and parity tests verify instruction presence, not model compliance or guaranteed preference persistence; live cross-provider adherence is unverified. No settings UI or runtime consent gate is added.

### Automatic verification and authorized landing

Implemented as version-controlled shared prompt guidance in `packages/core/src/agent/prompt.ts`, with regression coverage for prompt inclusion. For UI implementation tasks, agents should automatically proceed from automated checks to safely isolated visual/interaction testing, inspect matching baseline/after evidence and motion recordings, publish evidence under repository rules, and continue through review/checks to merge when landing is authorized and all gates pass. A draft PR or an unfinished-work note is not completion. Required verification plan steps remain pending/active while evidence is missing.

General policy belongs in the shared system prompt, not optional personal memory; repository-specific publication and merge conventions belong in `AGENTS.md`. This repository uses the single shared `pr-media` GitHub pre-release and verifies published PR media links. Existing host-side watch guidance is retained: register a bounded durable wake-up when waiting on background work, check status on wake, and report unavailable/failed registration honestly.

Safety boundaries: preserve user-owned instances and data, establish sandbox isolation before launching, do not bypass required checks/reviews/protections, and do not infer permission to merge in other repositories. Pause for concrete user-input, credential, authorization or safe-execution blockers, or explicit user scope limits; do not auto-resume around those blockers. Prompt tests verify instruction presence, not model compliance. No new runtime completion gate or guaranteed autonomous continuation is claimed; behavioral adherence across providers remains unverified. This is a non-visual policy change, so launching the app or capturing screenshots is not needed to verify this change.

The Rust daemon prompt (`crates/nekko-context/src/prompt.rs`) carries the same automatic-verification guidance. Host and daemon context golden tests verify shared instruction parity; prompt changes must update both implementations and regenerate the shared fixture against the changed core. This closes a coverage gap in the initial policy change, whose core-only tests did not catch daemon/fixture drift.

## Development macOS identity

Implemented and native smoke verified on 2026-10-06: `npm run dev` and desktop `start` prepare a separate, locally ad-hoc-signed **Nekko Agent.app** development bundle and launch it through macOS LaunchServices, rather than modifying the shared Electron download or launching directly under Terminal. The native display name is Nekko Agent. Development uses `com.nekkoagent.desktop.dev`, separate from the release identity. Existing macOS privacy grants are not reset or migrated; a disposable macOS runner verified the signed private bundle, preserved shared Electron runtime, Nekko Agent menu/Dock identity, microphone attribution, isolated data profile, owned shutdown/relaunch and full Command Center startup. The native screenshots were directly inspected. No user profile data or grants may be changed for testing. Other platforms retain the normal Electron launcher.

## Command Center header spacing

Implemented and verified in matched wide/narrow light/dark renderer captures plus native macOS title-bar clearance: the Command Center title and toolbar share the existing top row with a 4px top inset (previously 20px), reclaiming 16px for the wall below. Preserve native title-bar clearance, control sizes, horizontal gutters, and narrow-screen wrapping; do not move controls into the native draggable title bar.

### Focus chat, numbered icons and single approval surface

[Implemented; shared renderer visual and motion verification completed on 2026-10-06] Command Center chat headers combine the window number and a larger chat-outline icon instead of a separate numeric badge. Window numbers and Ctrl+1…9 selection keep their existing reading-order semantics. Permission approval controls appear on the chat transcript/full window only, never on a separate composer-only surface.

Focus mode places the agent-selection row above the focused window and uses the entire remaining height for one chat, including its composer. Other agents stay mounted offstage to preserve runs and drafts; switching agents updates the focus hero. Existing related workspace panes, including browsers, expand from the right edge of the focused chat as a rounded companion bubble while the chat contracts left. No new browser is launched or navigated merely by entering Focus; only already-open workspace panes are shown. Folding closes the companion bubble without closing workspace panes. Small widths stack companions below the chat; reduced-motion disables transitions. Acceptance requires single approval controls, preserved drafts across Grid/Focus switches, full-height focus layout, stable agent selection, and matching desktop screenshots plus motion evidence.

[Verified in isolated real ChatPane sandbox] Transcript-only panes never persist or clear composer drafts. Composer surface handoff flushes/restores text and images in layout lifecycle order; synthetic unsent text survived Grid → Focus → Grid. A synthetic pending approval rendered once across transcript/composer surfaces and approved once through a stub host; credentials, user sessions and command execution were not used. Full-app wide/narrow light/dark screenshots and companion folding motion were directly inspected. Grid/Focus handoff preserved text and an image attachment; Ctrl+2 and Ctrl+Tab selected the expected hero, one stubbed approval completed once, and reduced motion removed transitions. Narrow Focus reserves a scrollable 960px stage with stacked companions so transcript, approval and composer remain reachable. Native operating-system identity is outside this renderer change.
### Subscription quota direction

Implemented and verified with focused regression coverage and isolated Electron light/dark wide/narrow captures: Command Center shows ChatGPT subscription quota explicitly as **percent left** (`100 − used_percent`), matching remaining-quota conventions. Other provider windows explicitly say **percent used**. Meter fill and accessible value follow the visible meaning; warning colors still follow consumed quota (danger at 80% used). The host preserves the API's consumed percentage for routing and limit enforcement; no credential access, extra requests, or guessed account balance is introduced. Acceptance includes 17% used → 83% left, 83% used → 17% left, and zero/full boundaries. A screenshot alone cannot verify a live account's API value.

### Telling subscription accounts apart by email

`[implemented 2026-10-14; unit, golden and isolated renderer checks; live vendor sign-in unverified]` User intent: someone holding two plans on one vendor (for example a personal Claude Pro plan and a work one) sees two identical "Anthropic (Claude)" entries and cannot tell which account a model or a quota bar belongs to. Each signed-in provider now shows the email it is signed in as next to its name: `Anthropic (Claude) · davide@example.com`.

- **Where:** model picker (provider group headings, the provider tag on Recent/Starred/Default rows, and the chip's tooltip), the Command Center Utilization panel, and the sidebar Capacity list. Same for ChatGPT and any other sign-in that reports an email.
- **Source:** read once at sign-in from what the vendor already returns: Claude's token response `account.email_address`, ChatGPT's `id_token` `email` claim (the sign-in already asks for the `email` scope), and the Codex CLI import's `id_token`. Kept across token refreshes unless the vendor reports a new one. No extra request to the vendor.
- **Privacy boundary:** the email lives only in the host's `tokens.json` (mode 0600) next to the token, reaches the window only through the sanitized sign-in status (never with the token), and is not written to provider settings, so it never enters a settings export. Display only; it is never used to authorize anything. Values that do not look like an email are dropped.
- **Not covered:** API-key providers, OpenRouter and `claude setup-token` / pasted tokens carry no email and keep the bare label. Plans signed in before this change show no email until signed in again. A user-chosen account name is not offered yet (decided 2026-10-14: email only for now).
- **Verification:** TS and Rust token parsing held equal by the OAuth golden set (Claude with and without a valid email, ChatGPT `id_token` email, refresh keeping or replacing it, status, Codex import); render tests for the picker and Utilization. Whether Anthropic's live token response still includes `account.email_address` has not been checked against a real sign-in.

### Wall layout refinement (implemented and renderer verified)

The selected window has a softer accent halo with scroll-container padding on all sides so its top is not clipped. Grid's Add to the wall tile is a larger dashed card with a centered plus, rather than a shallow strip. Grid and Fixed use the same Add card width/height for the selected column/row preset and viewport; Grid places it below the saved split tree without changing split identities or ratios. Narrow layouts retain their shared stacked card dimensions. The composer occupies 75% of the available wall column on desktop, and full width on narrow screens. A horizontal separator between the wall and composer resizes the entire composer panel, not only the message input; double-click or Home resets its content-driven height. Left/right panel docks resize horizontally between 260 and 600px, snap within 20px of the recommended 368px width (408px before 2026-10-14), and reset with double-click or Home. Width and composer height adjustments are local to the mounted view, not persisted settings.

Panel headers carry icons and quiet icon-only minimize/remove controls with accessible names. Minimized panels move to a restore-icon row at the top of the panel area; restoring retains existing panel preference data. The icon entry respects reduced-motion preferences. Hardware uses compact labels and colored CPU/memory/GPU/memory bars with the existing percentages and exact used/total numbers below; disabled or unavailable probes never appear as measured zero. Command Center omits the floating monitor chip without disabling the existing shared sampler or monitor settings. No new external requests, probes, or user-data access are introduced.

Verified on 2026-10-06 with isolated full-app browser-edition fixtures: matching wide/narrow light/dark captures, directly inspected minimize/restore recording frames, pointer/keyboard dock and composer resizing, reset, focus handoff and reduced motion. The shared renderer uses existing theme tokens; no separate hardware target mockup was supplied. Native OS permission identity is outside this renderer change. At narrow widths the wall and dock share a scrollable column, preserving readable transcripts instead of squeezing the wall to zero height.

Dock minimization keeps the outgoing panel mounted for a 220ms shrinking transition before storing its minimized state and showing the restore icon. Reduced-motion users commit immediately; pending timers are cleared on unmount. Inspected motion evidence is published with the PR; keyboard focus follows the minimize and restore controls.
### Composer price estimates and unclipped model selection

[Implemented; shared renderer verified in isolated full-app browser fixtures on 2026-10-06] The composer displays the session's estimated API-equivalent cost, including subscription usage, rather than its zero per-token bill. Subscription billing remains $0; plan fees are excluded. Current published OpenAI standard short-context rates are matched explicitly; unknown variants are never assigned a guessed price. Unpriced subscription tokens show **No estimate** when none can be priced, or a **+** suffix on a partial total, with an explanation in the tooltip. Historical usage is recalculated from the pricing table without changing stored usage or credentials.

The model dropdown renders in a viewport-positioned portal, outside the chat pane's overflow clipping. It fits available height, scrolls its model list, clamps horizontally, and opens above or below the trigger according to available space. Inline empty-chat selection remains inline. Acceptance: subscription usage with published prices has a nonzero API-equivalent total while billed usage remains zero; unknown pricing is not presented as free; filtering, selection, favorites, outside-click dismissal and Escape continue working in narrow composer and full-chat windows. No new network requests or credential access are introduced by the picker.

The native display name is Nekko Agent. Development uses `com.nekkoagent.desktop.dev`, separate from the release identity. Existing macOS privacy grants are not reset or migrated; a disposable macOS runner verified the signed private bundle, preserved shared Electron runtime, Nekko Agent menu/Dock identity, microphone attribution, isolated data profile, owned shutdown/relaunch and full Command Center startup. The native screenshots were directly inspected. No user profile data or grants may be changed for testing. Other platforms retain the normal Electron launcher.

Questions and approvals remain inside the corresponding agent window, including when another chat is selected. Answering stays scoped to that session and does not grant additional permissions. The composer resize separator adds usable editor height rather than empty footer space; the wall and composer have no redundant column gap. Mounted isolated Electron checks verify cold-cache model loading, Auto selection persisted across reload, answers scoped to the owning window and usable editor height increasing with resize.
