## Command Center panel design parity (2026-10-06)

The side dock uses the app background with separate surface-colored panels, a 408px default width (20% wider), a visible Configure control and six-cell Vitals grid. Each panel has a pointer/keyboard vertical resize handle. Its maximum follows its measured content, including expanded pricing details; Home shrinks, End or double-click fits content. Panel sizes remain local to the mounted view. Narrow layouts retain the scrollable wall/dock column.

Utilization presents individual provider quota meters, retaining separate 5-hour and 7-day windows, reset countdowns and exact dates, available API credits, stale/unavailable states and recorded UTC token totals. Its spacing is compact (10px vertical padding, 8px section gaps, 6px provider gaps) without clipping quota details or changing other panels. An expired snapshot says “Quota may be outdated”; its tooltip gives the last update time and clarifies this is not a rate-limit warning. This label does not claim a refresh is in flight. Implemented with renderer regression coverage; matching compact-panel visual evidence remains pending. Budget shows UTC-month API spend, top API-spend agent and local tokens (all-time), plus separate all-time local and subscription API-cost equivalents. It does not ask for a monthly budget or show a target. Estimates exclude plan fees, electricity and hardware; unmatched subscription tokens remain explicitly unpriced.

Local models use matching published prices when available. Unpriced local models default to the clearly labeled Qwen3 32B cloud comparison ($0.08 input / $0.28 output per million tokens, DeepInfra via OpenRouter, verified 2026-10-06). Existing selected benchmarks remain honored; an empty selection opts out. Changes recalculate history and refresh the displayed totals. The comparison disclosure links pricing sources. No usage records or credentials are sent to pricing websites.

The layout selector sits to the left of the All/Agents/Terminals filter, orders Focus, Grid, Fixed and gives each a distinct SVG icon. The inline new-chat catalog is bounded to 320px and scrolls internally, keeping Create chat and wall panes clear of model rows. These follow-up changes are implemented with geometry tests and isolated Electron DOM checks (including a 41-model catalog); matching light/dark wide/narrow screenshots and a motion clip are captured but visual inspection remains unverified because the capture display surface is unavailable. Follow-up: subscription cost chips explicitly read `Subscription ($4.89)` (dynamic API-equivalent estimate, not an additional subscription bill); metered and local labels are unchanged. The wall column reserves at least 280px for the chat wall and scrolls when a tall composer cannot fit a short viewport, rather than collapsing the chat out of view. Isolated 1200×400 DOM checks verify visible wall height and no wall/composer overlap; subscription-label unit tests cover $4.89. Visual inspection and matching follow-up evidence remain required before landing. The synthetic fixture uses no real credentials, external connections or user profile data. Auto-arrange is an adjacent icon-only action with an accessible name; Panels has its own icon. Auto-add new agents is available only in Settings and preserves the rest of the saved wall configuration.

Verification: shared renderer web build and desktop typecheck pass; isolated wide/narrow light/dark fixtures and pointer/keyboard panel resizing inspected. Usage-accounting regression tests cover default comparison, exact Qwen/Ollama identifiers, opt-out and separate subscription/local amounts. Full desktop/daemon build also passed. Published evidence and CI/landing status are tracked in PR #345.

### Background visual evidence

Desktop chats prefer native app-window capture for visual evidence. Listing and capturing windows never raises, focuses, restores, or sends OS keyboard/mouse input to the target. Background capture of obscured, hidden, minimized or protected third-party windows depends on the OS and app; unavailable frames are reported without changing the user's workspace. Applications launched through arbitrary shell commands can still choose to activate themselves. Nekko does not promise to suppress those applications or OS permission dialogs.

A dedicated agent browser starts hidden, uses an isolated session, and stays hidden during navigation and DOM interactions. Explicit visibility shows it without activation. JavaScript dialogs are disabled; on Windows/macOS the owned window cannot receive OS focus, so its shown state is a preview and interactions use DOM actions. Linux retains normal focusability to avoid changing window-manager stacking. Its hidden PNG captures contain rendered page content, clearly identified as owned-page evidence; they do not prove native title bars, menus, Dock or OS dialogs. Actual native-window captures remain separately identified. Hidden owned windows support screenshots, while recordings require an OS-listed source.

Screenshots are saved to new paths inside the chat project. By default their pixels (up to 5 MB) are also attached to the selected chat model for inspection, with the disclosure included in capture approval. `inspect=false` saves locally without sending pixels. Vision support is required; Nekko never silently changes models or claims image inspection merely because a file was saved. Oversized images and videos remain saved evidence with explicit inspection limits. Approved screenshot attachments remain in the chat transcript under its normal retention policy. TypeScript and Rust provider adapters deliver evidence after all tool results in a batch, with captured text treated as untrusted content.

### Skill selection preserves in-progress prompts

[Implemented and manually verified] Adding an ordinary skill (including Plan) through the composer's **+ → Skill** menu arms or replaces the usage chip without changing any unsent text, Markdown, whitespace, line breaks, or pending image attachments. Removing the chip also leaves the draft intact. A skill selection alone never sends, queues, or starts work. A draft that is only a slash query (`/` plus one token) is consumed by any skill selection; Goal leaves `/goal `. Saved-prompt replacement behavior is unchanged. Selecting Goal through the + menu keeps the original draft as its condition after a single `/goal ` prefix, preserving existing background-task dispatch.

Acceptance: a multiline prompt remains byte-for-byte unchanged after adding Plan; replacing/removing an ordinary skill preserves text and images; text/images remain per-chat and survive composer handoff and draft save/restore; any skill selection still arms the skill without leaking a slash search query; Goal retains its condition. Selection introduces no additional storage, network calls, or external execution. The reported Plan draft-loss regression is covered by focused transition tests; manual verification by the user is recorded in PR #346.

## Agents dashboard simplification (2026-10-06)

Implemented and verified with isolated Windows Electron interactions and inspected matching light/dark, wide/narrow captures: Command Center is renamed Agents, with a stationary navigation cat. The former Agents workspace is Chat and is default-hidden behind Developer → Show Chat workspace. This is preparation for unification, not a merged workspace implementation. The corner mascot is blank-faced by default and occasionally opens/blinks; motion cycles are less frequent and respect reduced motion and hidden pages.

Utilization removes the outdated-quota note and offers an accessible refresh icon, requesting an authorized provider poll while retaining the 30-second provider throttle and showing errors. Composer removes its external-arrow shortcut and duplicate context/cost indicators; those stay in agent windows. Window Focus shortcut is removed; selection remains click-based. Online is a green indicator button toggling the owning session offline flag, without authorizing network use or changing other sessions. Hardware contains only CPU/memory/GPU/VRAM monitors. Dock Insights suppresses cost, savings/optimization, and model-cost details because Budget owns them. Side-dock panel headers support drag reorder and Alt+ArrowUp/Down; normalized order persists with existing settings and browser fallback.

Acceptance: matched main/current captures and sampled mascot/reorder motion frames were inspected. Chromium pointer drag/drop, saved keyboard ordering, owning-chat offline payloads and explicit provider refresh payloads pass isolated Electron checks. Grid and Fixed use available viewport height; Grid preserves saved splits and its separate scrollable Add row, with the same Add cell size as Fixed. The performance fixture uses the Agents navigation label and explicitly enables the optional Chat workspace; performance budgets remain unchanged. Provider refresh keeps existing credential boundaries; sandbox verification uses synthetic records and no external connections. Command Logs now disclose missing/failed snapshots and reactivate when commands append after a finished turn. New command output is persisted asynchronously beside session JSON; previously lost output cannot be recovered.

## Chat actions and persistent reply measurements

[Implemented; synthetic desktop payload checks and matched light/dark, wide/narrow visual evidence verified] Git checkout options sit below the explanatory notices and each starts with its worktree or branch icon. Chat transcript right-click menus provide Copy chat (the full Markdown transcript) and Export as Markdown; export is no longer a header button. Message Copy actions include a copy icon and To composer actions are removed. Completed persisted assistant replies show a checkmarked Done. subtext, excluding live, interrupted and tool-call messages. Reply measurements belong beneath the reply inside the scrolling chat transcript as subtle, faint text, not in the fixed actions footer or a separate bordered bar. They expose output tokens/s, total generated output tokens and elapsed time during a run and afterward; missing historical measurements are explicitly unavailable, never fabricated. This placement correction is implemented; matched light/dark, wide/narrow renderer verification is pending. It changes presentation only and adds no data collection or external requests. Model and effort controls have an explicit gap. Copy only writes to the clipboard on an explicit click, and export downloads locally. Acceptance requires clipboard/export interaction checks and matched wide/narrow light/dark desktop evidence. Historical measurement persistence remains unsupported by this renderer-only change.

### Grouped agent list in Agents (implemented; release verification pending)

Agents now hosts the existing Chat grouped sidebar, retaining WorkspaceCard presentation, project grouping, ordering, context actions, and each group's Show completed section (including groups with only completed chats). Active cards add/select their session on the Agents wall (Focus shows it as the hero; other layouts keep their arrangement, see the 2026-10-08 polish); sidebar chat, image and terminal creation stays in Agents. Completed cards open a read-only transcript in Agents with a Back to active agents action. Chat no longer renders the sidebar. Narrow viewports expose an Open agent list overlay. This reuses existing local summaries and archive retention; opening a card neither sends prompts nor restores completed chats. Acceptance requires isolated active/completed selection, project-only-completed coverage, resize/overlay interactions, and matching light/dark wide/narrow before/after evidence. Those release checks remain pending.

### Agents wall integration polish (2026-10-08)

`[Implemented; isolated renderer checks and matched dark/light desktop before/after evidence inspected. Narrow-width visual evidence, the real desktop title bar (native caption buttons), and macOS are unverified.]`

- **Agent panel.** The panel's hide button (a left side panel icon since round two) hides it, and an **Agents** button in the toolbar brings it back. The panel no longer repeats an "Agents" heading. An icon button switches between a vertical column beside the wall and a horizontal row above it, where groups lay their cards side by side in one sideways-scrolling strip (about one card tall) with the controls inline at its left. Visibility and orientation persist with the wall setting (`agentPanel`); invalid saved values fall back to a shown column. The panel sits directly on the Agents surface without the tinted frame that read as a stray border.
- **Opening from the panel.** In Focus, a card shows its chat as the hero. In Dynamic or Grid, the layout is kept: the chat's window is marked active, and a chat with no window on the wall is added to it first.
- **Layout names.** The saved modes keep their keys (`grid`, `fixed`) but read **Dynamic** (free tiled split tree) and **Grid** (even rows and columns). The shortcuts are unchanged.
- **Grid placeholders.** `[Implemented 2026-10-13; renderer-verified in the web edition]` In Grid, every cell of the chosen rows × columns that no window fills (and the rest of a partly filled overflow row) shows a very faint diagonal hatch with a dashed outline, so the grid's shape reads at a glance. Placeholders sit under the windows, take no clicks, and are not drawn on a one-column phone-width stack or an empty wall.
- **Board-edge landing zones.** `[Implemented 2026-10-13; renderer-verified in the web edition with synthetic drag events; a real pointer drag in the desktop app is unverified]` In Dynamic, dragging a window lights up a thin strip along each side of the whole board, not only the edges of other windows. Hovering one previews where the window would land ("New column" or "Full-width row"); dropping it makes the window a full-height column (left/right) or a full-width row (top/bottom). Two stacked windows become two columns by dropping the lower one on the right edge. With two columns, a third window dropped on the right becomes a third equal column and one dropped on the bottom becomes a wide row under both. A drop that would not change the layout shows no preview, a lone window offers no strips, and the eight-across ceiling still applies. Dropping onto another window's edges or centre works as before.
- **Header.** In the desktop shell the layout, filter, auto-arrange, panels and agent-panel controls render in the title bar row beside the Agents heading, kept clear of the window caption buttons. Without app chrome they stay in the in-view header.
- **Add window.** `[Placement superseded by round two below: centred under the wall, no arrow]` The bottom and right **+** edge rails are removed and the wall reserves no idle space for Add. A prominent **Add window** button sits to the right of the composer, with a dashed right-angle arrow running from it to the wall's bottom-right corner, where the new window lands. It opens the same inline Add to the wall slot and picker. Focus (which has no wall composer) keeps Add window in the toolbar, and an empty wall still offers Add inline.
- **Composer.** Prompt-analyzer suggestions are one **Suggestions** chip to the left of the microphone. Its details open in a popover above the chip instead of a bar above the editor. Pending image attachments and an armed skill add height to the wall composer instead of shrinking the editor. The wall composer's ring is drawn inside its clipped panel, removing the cut-off shadow band along the bottom and at the top corners.
- **Reply status.** A sleeping chat's "Sleeping · will check in N" label takes its own line, with the tok/s, total tokens and duration measurements on the line below.
- Privacy: UI-only preferences. Nothing here sends prompts, creates sessions (except an explicit Add or card open, as before), or contacts the network.

### Agents wall polish, round two (2026-10-08)

`[Implemented; isolated renderer checks and hidden-sandbox dark/light before/after evidence. Real desktop title bar, narrow widths, and macOS are unverified.]`

- **Empty wall illustration.** An empty wall centres an illustration (a wall of dashed window frames with Nekko sitting under it) above "The wall is empty" and the inline **Add a window** link. Special themes change it: **Spooky** (`autumn` preset) adds a cobweb, a bobbing bat (still under reduced motion), pumpkins and Nekko's wizard hat. Every other preset shows the plain variant. It is decorative (`aria-hidden`) and uses theme colours, so it reads in light and dark. A filter that hides every window still says "No windows match this filter." without the illustration.
- **Agents panel frame.** The Agents tab's panel draws no outer ring in either the vertical column or the horizontal row. The Agent tab's own sidebar keeps its ring.
- **Panel controls.** Hiding the panel uses a **left side panel** icon (replacing the ×), and the toolbar's **Agents** button that brings it back uses the same icon. Switching between the left column and the top row uses one icon: a frame with a curved two-headed arrow between its left side and its top.
- **New agent +.** The panel's **+** is drawn in the accent colour, a size larger. Its hover menu renders in a portal at the document root, positioned under the button, so the wall's windows and the panel's clipping no longer hide it.
- **Add window placement.** [Historical placement, superseded by the toolbar add bar and its 2026-10-13 anchored dropdown.] Add window sits centred directly below the wall, between the windows and the composer (above the wall, mirrored, when the composer docks on top). The elbow arrow is removed. Focus still keeps Add window in the toolbar.
- **Budget filters.** [Spend/token-only scope superseded 2026-10-13: all recorded Budget details now use the range.] The Budget panel has the same time-range filter as Insights' Tokens chart (today, 1wk, 1m, 6m, 1y, all-time; default 1m, UTC day buckets), filtering estimated spend and input/output tokens. Top agent and the savings estimates have no daily breakdown, so they stay all-time and say so.
- Privacy: UI-only. No new reads, permissions, network calls, or persisted settings (the Budget range is not saved).

### Agent window indicators and attention (2026-10-09)

`[Implemented; isolated renderer checks and hidden-sandbox dark/light before/after evidence. Real desktop window, narrow widths, macOS, and a live question/approval from a real agent run are unverified.]`

- **Needs you, again.** A wall window waiting on you (a question, a permission approval, or a turn that stopped part-way) wears a warm 2px ring with a soft pulsing glow, selected or not and in every layout; reduced motion keeps the ring and drops the pulse. This had regressed in 2026-10-04's chat-window rework, which kept only the strip tint. The window's status shows **?** (needs input) or the warning glyph. In the Agents panel a card now shows **?** for a question as well as an approval, keeps it while the parked run streams, and clears it once answered; a chat already waiting when the panel mounts is read from the host (`pendingInput`).
- **Status glyph placement.** A chat window's status glyph (rocket working, Zz idle, ?, warning) sits in the far bottom-right corner of the window's footer instead of the title strip. Terminals keep their strip dot.
- **Internet toggle.** The footer's "Online" label is an icon toggle: a globe when this agent may reach the internet, the globe struck through when it is blocked. The tooltip says that clicking allows or blocks internet connectivity for this agent. Blocking is the chat's existing Offline mode (no tools, connectors or network). As before, only a chat on a local model can be blocked: on a cloud model the toggle is disabled and its tooltip says why.
- **Tools and MCP in the window.** The Tools and MCP pickers moved from the composer's control row to the agent window's footer, beside the internet toggle. Their menus float above the window (portalled) so the window's clipping cannot cut them off. The composer keeps Mode, Incognito, Thinking and Automate; its Offline chip is replaced by the window's internet toggle.
- **Composer.** The wall composer no longer shows the "Continue work" / "Retry" suggestion box. A turn that actually stopped still shows its error banner with Retry in the agent window.
- **Chat panel groups.** Closing a group hides its cards completely: they leave the tab order (`inert`), cannot be clicked, and in the horizontal row the group's strip takes no width. In the row the mouse wheel scrolls sideways. In the column the card list scrolls with the wheel inside the panel, which is bounded by the wall's height.
- Privacy: the internet toggle only flips the existing per-chat `offline` option. No new reads, permissions or network calls.

### Agent questions, plan toggle, and dock sign-in (2026-10-09)

`[Implemented; isolated renderer checks and hidden-sandbox dark/light before/after evidence. A live agent question, a real OAuth sign-in from the dock, the real desktop window and macOS are unverified.]`

- **Agent questions stand out and stay on top.** When an agent window is waiting on a question, the question is pinned at the top of the window, above the transcript, so it cannot scroll away or sit under the history. It opens with a bold warning-toned "Asked you a question" label, and the card itself wears the warning tone (stronger border, tinted background, soft halo) with the question text a size larger. The pin is capped at about 60% of the window and scrolls inside itself. The old strip banner, which sat underneath the window's absolutely positioned content and could not be seen, is removed. Other surfaces (the composer's question, board cards) keep the accent tone.
- **Plan panel toggle.** The plan / sub-agents / queue panel no longer has a close (×) button or a header toggle. A plan icon floats in the chat area's top-right corner and opens or closes only its correlated chat's panel; with the panel closed, it shows plan progress (done/total). The panel is on by default (a saved choice still wins) and is fixed at 280px. It only gives way when the window is narrower than two panels (560px), where the toggle is hidden too. Beside the open panel the transcript keeps clear of the toggle. Inside the panel, Sub-agents come first, then the Agent plan, then queued follow-ups ("After this"), so delegated work in flight is the first thing visible `[shipped 2026-10-13]`.
- **Sign in again from Utilization.** When a subscription's sign-in has expired or is missing, the Utilization panel says "Sign-in expired." (or "Not signed in.") with a **Sign in again** button right there, instead of "Sign in again in Settings". It opens the same sign-in flow as the provider card (browser OAuth, paste fallback, CLI import) and folds the new token onto that provider, then refreshes the quota.
- Privacy: the sign-in uses the existing OAuth flow and token store; the access token never reaches the renderer.

### Consistent end-of-request summary, incognito icon (2026-10-09)

`[Implemented; prompt text unit-tested in TS and in the Rust parity golden. Whether each provider's model actually follows the format is unverified and will vary.]`

- **One closing shape for every model.** The shared system prompt (`packages/core/src/agent/prompt.ts`, mirrored in `crates/nekko-context/src/prompt.rs`) now tells every model how to close a turn that changed anything (files, commits, branches, PRs, settings, running processes): a short **Changes** list (one bullet per change, naming the files or PR touched, linked when available), then **Verified**, **Not verified / blocked** ("Nothing" only when true), and the concrete **Next step**, with those bold labels. Conversation, questions and answers, and turns that changed nothing stay plain replies. This builds on the existing honest wrap-up rule; the app does not parse or enforce the format.
- **Incognito icon.** The incognito toggle uses the familiar hat-and-sunglasses glyph browsers use for private windows, replacing the old mask.
- **Wall title bar observer.** The Agents controls' title bar slot is re-found by watching only its ancestors (`childList`, no subtree) rather than every DOM change in the window, so streamed transcript text on the wall no longer runs the observer.
- **Reply stats on the Done row (2026-10-09).** A finished reply's measurements (model, effort, tokens in/out, tok/s, duration) sit right-aligned on the same row as "Done.", always at least 30px clear of it; when the row is too narrow they wrap onto the next line, still right-aligned. A reply without a Done (interrupted, or ending on tool calls) keeps the stats on their own line as before. `[Implemented; renderer tests and hidden-sandbox wide/narrow dark/light checks.]`
- **Logs on the Agents wall (2026-10-09; the bubble is superseded 2026-10-11 by the Logs drawer, see "Default folders for new agents and the Logs drawer").** Bug: the agent window's Logs button opened a terminal pane in the Chat view, which is hidden unless the developer Chat surface is on, so the app bounced straight back to Agents and nothing appeared. On the wall, Logs now bubbles the agent's read-only command log out of the middle of that window's right edge (anchored at the edge's midpoint and grown from it; reduced motion skips the animation). Esc, the close button, a click outside, or Logs again closes it; when the Chat view is enabled the bubble also offers to pop the log out as its own window. Outside the wall, Logs opens the log beside the chat as before. `[Implemented; renderer tests and hidden-sandbox dark/light checks of placement and the stay-on-wall behaviour.]`
- **Wall chat switching (2026-10-09).** Each wall window's status glyph is passed to its memoized chat pane as a value rather than a freshly created element, so selecting a chat no longer re-renders every other window on the wall. `[Implemented; local profile shows the other windows no longer re-render on a switch; CI perf result on the PR.]`

### Former Command Center agent tabs (superseded by the grouped list)

- Creating an agent from the wall's New agent controls selects the new session and its focus window/composer without leaving Command Center.
- Right-click the agent tab bar to choose Top tabs, Vertical tabs (left), or Hide tab panel. An always-visible Agent tabs control provides the same menu and restores hidden tabs.
- Tab placement persists with command-wall settings and browser fallback, independently of focus/grid/fixed layout. Hidden tabs retain sessions and Ctrl+Tab/number shortcuts.
- Below 720px, the left preference renders as a horizontal strip to preserve editor width; the saved preference remains left.
- Acceptance: new-agent selection, all placement choices, right-click access, recovery, and setting/local/reload persistence pass isolated Electron checks. Desktop/light/dark and narrow visual evidence must be inspected before release.
- Privacy: placement changes only UI settings; they neither send prompts nor create/delete sessions.

Native iOS first-run sizing: the generated Xcode project consumes the checked-in Info.plist directly so the launch storyboard survives generation and iPhone does not enter legacy letterboxed compatibility mode. CI checks the built launch-screen metadata and compiled storyboard before simulator captures.

### October 7 integration verification

[Historical behavior, superseded by the 2026-10-13 background notification and panel batch above.] The inline creation picker remains inside its Add tile, with a 64px heading and measured content height, while preserving the compact edge rails and preview behavior. The existing wall panes retain their zero top offset. Focused wall tests and isolated full-view creation, retry, dismissal, keyboard and reduced-motion checks pass. Native iOS remains a read-only direct-HTTPS development foundation: protocol, ad hoc signed simulator Keychain/host-binding and first-run UI tests pass. The generated app retains its launch storyboard and renders full-height in inspected light/dark simulator captures. Live authenticated hosts, physical devices, iPad, encrypted pairing/relay, send/approvals, inference and signed distribution remain separate acceptance gates tracked in #360.
### Retry and model-change context transparency (2026-10-07)

[Implemented; isolated renderer interactions and matched visual evidence verified] A pending ask_user question is a normal waiting state, not an interrupted reply. Persisted-interruption detection and the recovery banner are suppressed while that question is pending. Recovery exposes one non-destructive **Retry** action (formerly Continue); Start over is removed. Retry resumes against saved conversation/work and displays approximate input-context tokens from included Context Inspector items, or explicitly unavailable when no bundle exists. This is not a billable-token or cost guarantee: live changes, tool schemas, compaction, truncation, provider tokenization and caching can alter the request or charges.

Changing model or global effort in an existing chat opens an informational dialog after saving the selection. No request is sent merely by selecting, and a running reply is not restarted. Subsequent requests assemble context as ordinary follow-ups do; a different model may not reuse provider caches. The dialog suggests a new chat with only necessary information to reduce input context. Effort remains global. No context is transmitted by the notice itself; existing provider consent/routing boundaries remain unchanged. Acceptance: question waits have no recovery banner; one Retry preserves saved work; estimates are clearly approximate; model/effort changes explain next-request semantics and the new-chat alternative. Windows Electron verification covers matched light/dark wide/narrow captures, deterministic assembled-context estimates, saved-transcript retry payloads including no-progress failures, Escape/Tab behavior and focus return. The recovery notice wraps across rows at narrow widths instead of squeezing its explanation into a thin column. Native clipboard/export delivery and real provider billing are separate follow-ups.
## Prompt caching across providers (2026-10-07)

[Implemented; automated provider/host/daemon parity and Windows managed-runtime checks verified; live cloud billing unverified.] Settings > Agent loop offers a persisted Prompt caching switch, enabled for new installs and existing settings that omit the field. Switching it does not shorten context, omit history or tool results, reuse answers, or hide file/memory changes. Every request continues to carry its complete assembled context.

Native cache preparation marks reusable prefixes for Anthropic, OpenRouter Claude, and conservatively supported OpenRouter Gemini text models. OpenAI/ChatGPT and compatible automatic-caching services retain their full requests and expose cache counts only when the service reports them. Ollama and externally managed local servers retain their automatic KV behavior; unsupported remote endpoints are not treated as explicitly cached. Cache thresholds, retention, routing and actual hits remain provider-controlled. Specialized or unknown Gemini models are not given speculative cache directives.

Off removes Nekko explicit cloud cache requests and disables managed llama.cpp request reuse. Managed launch policy changes take effect when the model reloads, using only flags supported by the installed binary. External providers may still cache automatically. Only the current host-owned endpoint receives managed cache controls, never a persisted assertion on an arbitrary endpoint. KV reuse remains in RAM/GPU memory; this feature adds no disk prompt/KV store, external service, or additional prompt recipient. Cloud caches follow the selected provider retention policy.

Usage records and live turn prices distinguish noncached input, cache reads and cache writes, without inventing missing counters. When the engine daemon drives a turn it writes that turn's usage and reply lines itself, in the TS host's exact format, and keeps the subscription-limit snapshots (header capture, throttled polls, failure backoff, quota history) and serves the usage summary; the TS host only renews sign-ins for it and relays limit changes to the phone. Without a daemon the TS services keep doing all of it. `[implemented 2026-10-12, PF14]` Historical records without cache fields retain their existing meaning. TS and Rust loops, sideband calls and supported daemon contracts carry the setting and counters; older daemons fall back to the current TS path. Observed sideband usage is preserved on success, errors and timeouts. Accounting remains best effort across crashes, lost upstream counters, orphaned daemon events lacking attribution, and failed persistence; exactly-once recovery is not implemented.

Acceptance verified: full-context on/off payload tests; provider request/stream golden parity; legacy default-on and explicit-off persistence; cache-aware pricing including read-only/write-only events; isolated Settings on/off remount checks at desktop/narrow widths in light/dark; Windows managed llama.cpp repeats reused 1368 of 1369 prompt tokens with caching on and zero with caching off. The local result is not a promise of cloud quota savings or an explanation of any particular subscription percentage. Live billable cloud requests, Mac/Linux runtime behavior and production engine-catalog integration remains unverified and must be checked before claiming those outcomes.
### Persistent session command logs

Implemented with focused host and renderer tests: `<session-id>.commands.log` sidecars live beside `<session-id>.json` in local session storage. Live terminal events remain immediate; ordered asynchronous appends retain raw command output and ANSI sequences without rewriting session JSON. Snapshot restoration reads only the last 256KB from disk, including when the backend has no live terminal state; appending after restart retains old history. Disk history is append-only without automatic size rotation. Session deletion queues sidecar removal after pending appends and prevents late writes from recreating it. Session discovery ignores sidecars. Disk failures are logged and snapshot reads surface an error rather than implying successful persistence.

Privacy: files contain potentially sensitive command output, remain local with the same data-root access boundaries as sessions, and request owner-only creation permissions where supported. No automatic upload or redaction. Already-lost historical logs cannot be recovered. Pending asynchronous writes can be lost on abrupt process termination; no fsync or power-loss durability guarantee. Desktop backend graceful shutdown drains pending log writes before exit; abrupt termination remains best effort. Process-restart integration and large-log verification are required; filesystem restoration and write-order/deletion/error paths have focused tests. No layout or styling changes in this persistence batch; only the missing-output explanatory text changes.

### Selected native window capture

Native screenshot enumeration requests no thumbnails. Only the chat-selected window is granted to an isolated hidden, non-focusable capture helper, which encodes one native video frame as PNG and stops the stream. This avoids capturing unrelated windows and their Windows Graphics Capture errors. Acquisition and first-frame waits are bounded; unavailable targets fail without restoring or focusing them, changing capture permissions, or substituting another source. Hidden owned-page capture remains separate.

### Background verification

Routine development verification preserves the active desktop: isolated renderer fixtures stay hidden or off-screen and non-focusable, including timed motion captures. Native capture fixtures remain off-screen and non-focusable. Tests requiring real foreground branding or OS permission dialogs run on a disposable CI desktop; local foreground tests require an explicit user request. Hidden evidence is renderer evidence, not proof of native chrome. Minimized third-party capture remains OS-dependent and must not silently restore the target.

## Grid model selection and chat-mode menus (2026-10-07)

A model chosen in a Grid chat must immediately become that chat's shared composer's selection when the save completes. Sending from the shared composer uses the chosen provider/model for Claude and built-in Nekko Server models, including an empty new conversation, without asking the user to pick the same model again. Model changes are scoped to the owning chat and persist across layout changes/reloads. Failed saves show an error.

The Mode control (Ask, Guardrails, YOLO) opens a viewport-contained menu outside the Grid composer's scrolling/clipping containers. Pointer selection persists the owning chat's mode; Escape dismisses it and returns focus. Grid and Focus retain the same policies and privacy controls. No credentials, inference requests or tool executions occur merely from changing a model or opening a mode menu.

Local provider streaming accepts LF and CRLF framing, including line endings split across network chunks. OpenAI-compatible and Ollama stream error payloads surface their reason instead of silently ending. TypeScript and Rust providers share regression expectations. A loaded 3B Nekko Server model producing no GPU/memory activity is consistent with the Grid request being blocked before inference, but that specific machine's inference remains unverified; no hardware-capacity cause is asserted.

The send-cat control has a 4px right margin. Desktop builds with app chrome omit the duplicate brand row above navigation; web builds without chrome retain their identity row. The Agent navigation icon itself remains. Acceptance: Add hover/focus never reflows cards, click opens creation; composer and send controls remain reachable in wide/narrow light/dark layouts. No new reads, network access, permissions, or changes to persisted user data.

PR 364 follow-up: width observers consume delivered ResizeObserver widths rather than forcing another layout read; cold mounting still performs one bootstrap read. Focused width/cache/cleanup regression checks pass. This does not establish the cause or resolution of CI streaming/cold-frame budget misses; final unchanged-budget CI measurements remain required. Local capture-tool inspection, sidebar visual evidence and motion inspection are deferred by explicit user request, not marked verified.

Implemented; verification pending: remove the agent/terminal counts subtitle and show Agents beside the desktop Nekko Agent brand; browser/mobile retain the in-view heading. The height drag handle sits immediately against the composer and follows its width and alignment. Both side handles resize the composer between 320px (or the available narrower width) and the wall-column width. Center docking resizes symmetrically; left/right docking retains its anchor. ## Live streaming token rate

Implemented, focused regression verification complete; visual/runtime-provider verification pending. Agent reply stats display an approximate (~) tokens/sec rate during a current model call before provider usage arrives, using streamed text/reasoning character counts (four characters per token) and the first-to-latest chunk interval. The first chunk/interval under 100ms remains unavailable rather than inventing a rate. Prompt latency, tool execution and retry backoff are excluded by starting fresh timing windows; failed retry output is discarded. Usage clears the estimate and restores the provider-measured turn rate. No per-token parent ChatPane updates, extra provider requests or persisted telemetry are added. Acceptance: a multi-chunk stream shows a labeled estimate before usage; measured rates remain authoritative on completion; tool/retry waits do not dilute the next call rate.

## Repository review integration

[Configured; activation and live review unverified] CodeRabbit is intended to automatically review draft and ready pull requests and incremental pushes for the public nekko-agent repository. The root .coderabbit.yaml uses quiet actionable review guidance aligned with AGENTS.md, protects the PR description from automatic summaries, and disables automatic approval and code-generating finishing touches. Reviews are advisory and never replace required CI, human review, or inspected UI evidence. Acceptance requires repository-scoped GitHub App installation, confirmation of the free public-repository offering with paid add-ons disabled, and an observed real review plus an incremental update. No credential or Actions secret is added; hosted review processes public code and PR context under CodeRabbit terms. Access to unrelated/private repositories or external MCP integrations requires separate approval. See reports/code-review-integrations.md for owner activation steps, fair-use limits and complementary tools.
## Minimize agent windows

Implemented; focused action verification pending, visual verification deferred by user direction. Each chat window on the Agents wall offers a minus control immediately before its configured lifecycle action (Complete by default). Minimize removes only the pane from the wall tree; it does not archive/complete/delete the chat or abort its running reply. The incomplete chat remains in the left Agents list and selecting it restores its window. Drafts and session records remain intact; no new host actions or permissions. Terminal and non-wall workspace windows do not gain this control. Acceptance: minimize invokes only pane removal, Complete remains independent, and sidebar restore retains incomplete state.

## Agent activity and panel polish

Implemented; focused checks pending, visual/motion verification deferred by user direction. Working rockets are16px and success-green. Wall agent titles use a leading8px status dot (green working, warning-orange waiting, muted idle) instead of status words. Remove header child-count chip: active direct subagents appear in a bottom-right floating robot/count/status control within their chat window; expanding lists active children and selecting adds/reuses their wall window. Clicking outside the control or pressing Escape dismisses the popup. No completion or provider side effects. Side dock headers retain drag/drop and Alt+Arrow reorder with visible grip; default order is Hardware, Utilization, Budget, Automations, Insights (Vitals led, and was removed from the dock, on 2026-10-13). Saved custom orders remain intact. Minimize uses a shared standard minus line glyph, replacing download icon. Source reference: https://lucide.dev/icons/minus (standard shape, local SVG with no dependency/network at runtime). Acceptance: activity colors/size, title-dot placement, expandable child-window routing, panel order persistence and independent minimize/complete behavior.

Post-merge PR367 performance follow-up: shared wall composer editor height remains flex/CSS-owned; generic draft sizing does not measure or rewrite that surface. Full chat sizing reveals a caret only for its focused editor. No limits, drafts, permissions or network behavior change. Focused regression coverage added; unchanged CI performance budgets remain pending.

### Conversation plan history and window vacancies

Implemented; focused regression tests and isolated renderer interaction checks pass. Matching published before/after screenshots at 1400px/480px in light/dark and sampled video frames at 0.1/0.5/1.0/1.6 seconds were inspected in an isolated browser. They confirm matching option backgrounds, bottom-right popup placement and vertical vacancy growth without left-column widening. This verifies synthetic renderer behavior, not native OS chrome or real provider integration. The question prompt's Something else row uses the same background as other unselected options and the same highlighted surface when hovered by keyboard. The subagent control stays bottom-right even while expanded. Clicking outside or pressing Escape closes it; no running children hides and resets the popup.

Removing or completing a window in aligned tiled rows prefers growing its vertical neighbor instead of widening the card to its left. Existing column widths and row proportions remain stable. Irregular or spanning rows retain conservative split removal rather than moving unrelated cards.

Agent plans accumulate settled conversation history. Replacement retains done and skipped steps, matches repeated titles without duplicating them, and appends genuinely new work. It can revise unfinished scope; explicit status updates can revisit an existing step. Reused generated IDs cannot overwrite unrelated completed history. Ordinary chats and goal runs share these semantics. No new permissions, provider calls, telemetry or external content disclosure are introduced. Acceptance: matching question backgrounds, stable bottom-right popup with dismissal, vertical vacancy growth without left-card widening, and completed history retained across later requests.

Explicitly opening Add window from the Focus toolbar or a focused window switches to Dynamic, so new windows can be placed alongside the current one. Hover previews do not change layout. ### Composer-side Add and resilient toolbar (2026-10-08)

`[Placement superseded 2026-10-10 by the toolbar add bar below]` Add window sits to the right of the shared composer in the same row, including top docking and narrow layouts, instead of reserving a separate vertical row. Focus retains its toolbar Add. The desktop toolbar observes replacement of its titlebar host and reattaches rather than retaining a detached portal. Simple Agents/Chat navigation did not reproduce the reported toolbar loss; host replacement is a covered recovery case, not a confirmed original cause. No new permissions or external effects. Implemented and verified with 72 focused tests, source-aware desktop typecheck, and isolated hidden Electron checks for same-row placement at 1400px/400px, top docking, resized composer, picker activation, repeated Agents/Chat navigation, responsive toolbar relocation, and replaced-host recovery. ### Toolbar add bar replaces Add window (2026-10-10)

Adding to the wall is a row of icon buttons at the start of the Agents toolbar's controls (left of the layout switcher, moved there the same day from the right end), in every layout: **New agent** (the Agents cat), **New image session** (a tilted instant photo, Nekko's own line icon rather than the stock picture glyph; added 2026-10-13, it starts an image chat beside the biggest window and selects it), **New terminal**, then **Changes**, **Browser** and **Files** for the selected agent, then **More** (⋮). More opens the existing Add-to-the-wall picker: chats already running, an image session, or a new chat with details. Changes, Browser and Files open as companions in the selected agent's own workspace (made if it has none), which the wall shows beside that agent's window, unfolded (superseded 2026-10-13: from an agent window's own header they open as a wall drawer, like Logs); they never switch away from the Command Center, keep one of each per chat, and are disabled until an agent is selected. The Add window button beside the composer is gone, so the composer row holds only the composer. The empty wall's "Add a window" link still opens the picker. Implemented and verified with focused store and toolbar tests, the full desktop suite, the grid integration check, and isolated before/after captures in light and dark (web edition, scratch data, Playwright Chromium).

### Agents wall: new-chat steps, status glyphs and composer chrome (2026-10-08)

Implemented, not release-verified. Focused unit tests, build:web and workspace typecheck pass with worktree-local package resolution. Hidden network-blocked synthetic renderer captures cover 1400px and 600px, light and dark; visual inspection is still required with an image-capable tool. Native window chrome and real provider/folder integration are not exercised. The original second-title root cause remains unconfirmed.

- **New chat in Add window is three steps and one Start.** 1 *Model and effort*: the chosen model reads back in a summary row (saved default model, else the active model, else the active provider's first, so it is preselected); *Change* opens the list, picking a model highlights it and closes the list, and the effort slider sits beneath. 2 *Folders*: checkboxes for the project folders, the current project preselected; clearing every checkbox starts without a working folder. Only enabled providers supply active-model defaults, and the fallback model must belong to that provider. The first ticked is the chat's primary working folder and the others are attached as supporting folders. 3 *Open an existing chat instead*: an optional collapsed dropdown. **Start** creates the chat with these choices; with defaults in place, Start is one click. Effort remains the app-wide setting it already was, not a per-chat value.
- **Wall windows show status as a glyph, not a dot.** A chat window's strip and the wall composer header show the green rocket while the agent works and "Zz" when idle (question mark when it needs input, warning when an interrupted turn stopped it), matching the sidebar. Terminal windows keep their live/exited dot.
- **One title per wall window.** A chat on the wall never draws its own header row under the window strip; the strip already names it.
- **No box behind the wall composer.** The composer's bottom information strip carries no border or background in the wall composer, where it only ever hosts the pending-PR dock, so an empty strip no longer shows as a band below the composer.
- **Delegation errors name the fix.** A sub-agent call that sends `provider_id`/`model_id` as empty strings is still refused (no child, no fallback), but the error now tells the model to omit them to inherit the chat's route or to call `delegation_targets` first. A failed target model-list check is logged with the provider ID and error class only, never the message (which can quote endpoints or tokens).

### Wall drawers, the dock and the dev app's name (2026-10-13)

- **Changes, Files and Browser open as wall drawers.** On the Agents wall, the Changes, Files and Browser buttons in an agent window's header slide out of the window's right edge in the same drawer as Logs, at the same size, and clicking the same button again (or ×, Esc) closes it; another of the four switches the drawer in place. They used to open as companions under a window that was folded away on a busy wall, so the button looked dead. In the Chat view they still open as windows of the chat's workspace. The drawer's one-shot slide is exempt from the pause that holds looping decorations while the app is unfocused (paused on its first frame it was fully clipped, and a closing one never unmounted).
- **The Browser works in the web edition.** Electron shows pages in a `<webview>`; the web edition has none and now uses a sandboxed iframe (the renderer's CSP allows http(s) frames for it). The address bar follows in-page navigation (its listeners were attached before the webview existed and never fired).
- **Logs drawer at 96%** of its window's height.
- **The Agents dock leads with Hardware** by default, and the **Vitals** dock panel is gone (saved docks drop it on load). The Insights window keeps its own Vitals strip.
- **`npm run dev` on Windows runs as Nekko Agent**, with the app icon and name in the taskbar and Task Manager, instead of Electron: the dev launcher makes a cached copy of the Electron runtime per version as `Nekko Agent.exe` with the icon and version strings patched (macOS already ran a renamed dev bundle).
- **The wall composer starts two lines tall** and keeps its dragged size across tabs and restarts (see Command Center: the wall).

`[Implemented; unit tests, the full desktop suite, and isolated web-edition before/after captures (dark, 1600x1000, headless Playwright Chromium) with real pointer drags for the composer. The Electron window's taskbar identity was checked from the patched executable's version info and icon, not by launching the app on screen.]`

### Default folders for new agents and the Logs drawer (2026-10-11)

- **Default folders.** The Folders step of a new chat in Add window can save the folders that are ticked as the default for every new agent: "Set this folder as default" for one, "Set these N folders as default" for several. One or many can be the default; their order is kept and the first is the primary working folder, the rest supporting. Saved defaults are preticked the next time and carry a Default badge; "Your default folders" shows when the ticks match them, and **Clear default** goes back to preticking the current project's folder. A chat started without the picker (the toolbar's New agent, a window's split) also starts with the defaults; elsewhere in the app a new chat takes the first default as its folder. A default folder that has been removed is ignored. Stored as `defaultWorkspaceIds` in settings; no new permissions, host calls or folder access. `[Implemented; unit tests, isolated web-edition captures of the picker saving two defaults.]` `[updated 2026-10-13]` **Star a folder as default from any folder list.** The composer's folder menu and the side panel's Folders list put a star on each folder: a filled star marks a default, clicking an empty star adds that folder to the defaults (appended, so the first starred stays primary), and clicking a filled star removes it. The side panel shows the star on hover, and always for a starred folder. It writes the same `defaultWorkspaceIds` the Add window picker writes, so both stay in step. `[Implemented; unit tests for the toggle order and the menu markup. Desktop visual check pending.]`
- **Logs opens as its own drawer beside the window.** Supersedes the 2026-10-09 bubble, which floated over the window and covered the transcript. On the Agents wall, Logs slides the command log out of the window's right edge as a drawer of its own: 96% of the window's height (90% until 2026-10-13), centred on it, with no border on its left side, joining the window as one shape (the window gives up its right-hand rounding while it is open). Windows to its right in the same rows move right to make room and are squeezed into the rest of the wall (no narrower than 240px; the drawer narrows first, down to 280px), so nothing is pushed off; windows in other rows stay put. When the window is the rightmost one, the window itself narrows. Closing (×, Esc, Logs again) absorbs the drawer back into the window while the neighbours slide back. One drawer is open at a time; it closes if its window leaves the wall, in Focus's deck, or on a phone-width wall. Reduced motion skips the animation. Outside the wall, Logs still opens the log beside the chat. `[Implemented; layout unit tests, isolated web-edition before/after captures and a motion recording (dark theme). Light theme and the Electron desktop window are not separately captured.]`

- **No bar above the composer.** The row naming the agent the composer speaks for (its numbered agent icon, title, status, and the Focus and dock-position buttons) is now the composer card's own first row, replacing the controls strip that was there. Automate sits at that row's right end. With no agent selected the same row stands alone over the empty state.

### Agents wall polish, round three (2026-10-08)

Implemented; focused tests and isolated renderer captures verified (dark/light, 1400/760 px, panel as column, row and collapsed). The orientation-switch animation is implemented (FLIP, 280 ms, off under reduced motion) but its motion is not captured as video.

- **Composer resize grips** float in the gap just outside the composer (14 px out on the left, right and top/bottom edges) instead of sitting on its border, and grow and take the accent on hover or keyboard focus. The composer row gained enough side padding that they are never clipped.
- **Working agent windows** show a quieter copy of the composer's spinning border beam while their agent works: thinner, 55% opacity, softer colours, a slower lap. Idle, waiting and terminal windows have none.
- **Auto-arrange** reveals by its own width instead of a fixed 38 px, which was narrower than the button in the title bar, so the wand no longer overlaps the All/Agents/Terminals filter beside it. `[updated 2026-10-12]` Its space is always reserved: picking Dynamic only fades and scales the wand in place, so the filter and Panels never shift when the layout mode changes. Verified by hidden renderer measurement (filter and Panels positions identical across Focus → Dynamic → Grid → Focus); not yet seen in the user's running app.
- **One context number per chat** `[updated 2026-10-12]`. The sidebar card's context readout (e.g. "81k/1M") and the composer's context gauge show the same total for an open chat: every included context item (system prompt, tools, guidelines, memory, conversation) plus what a running turn has produced. The card used to count only the conversation, and only while the chat was working, so it read a few thousand tokens under the gauge (76k vs 81k). The unsent draft and an armed skill stay out of the card's number until sent; the gauge still includes them as you type. A chat that is not open falls back to its transcript estimate. Only the pane that draws the gauge publishes: the Agents view opens the selected chat a second time in the wall composer, which has no gauge and dropped a finished reply's tokens, and when both published the card read thousands under the gauge (94k vs 100k). Verified by unit tests (card mirrors the published total idle and working; falls back when closed; composer copy does not publish) and a hidden Agents-view fixture replaying a full turn (gauge 2.9k, card 2,935 after the reply; before the fix the card fell back to 231); not yet seen in the user's running app.
- **Agents panel controls** live only in the panel: the title bar no longer has the Agents and orientation buttons. They are icon pills that grow their label ("Hide agents", "Row", "Column") out of the icon on hover or focus. In a column panel they lie in a row across its top; in a row panel they stack at its left. Collapsing the panel leaves them where it was (a strip down the wall's left, or a row above it), offering "Show agents". Changing orientation slides each button from its old place to its new one.
- **Agent window icon**: each wall window and the composer header show a small robot head whose face is the window's keyboard number, replacing the chat bubble. It takes the accent when the window is selected or working.

### Quota read reasons, unavailable models and kept reply stats (2026-10-08)

Implemented; focused host/renderer tests and isolated renderer captures verified. Live provider behaviour (a real 429, a real token-endpoint rejection) is exercised only through mocked responses.

- **Why a quota is missing.** A failed subscription quota read records a reason (`LimitsProblem`): not signed in, sign-in expired (401/403, a rejected token refresh, or a ChatGPT sign-in without an account id), rate limited (429, honouring Retry-After), another HTTP status, unreachable, or an unreadable answer. The Utilization panel says so ("Sign-in expired. Sign in again in Settings.", "Rate limited by the provider. Retrying at 14:05.") with a Manage providers link for sign-in problems, instead of "Quota unavailable." The reason is read over `limits:problem` only after an empty answer. Codes and counts only: no token, URL or response body is stored or shown.
- **Backoff.** After a failure the host waits 1 minute, doubling per consecutive failure up to 30 minutes, or longer when Retry-After says so; the existing 30 second per-account throttle still applies on top. An expired or missing sign-in stops quota requests entirely until the stored token changes (a new sign-in, or a chat's own refresh succeeding). A success clears the reason. Polling cadence (60 s idle, 30 s while a subscription chat runs) is unchanged.
- **Unavailable model.** When a chat's saved model is not in its provider's list (for example a signed-out subscription), the model chip keeps naming it, struck through and marked "unavailable" in the warning colour, instead of a floating "Choose a model" popup. The floating popup is removed everywhere. A one-line note ("gpt-6.1-sol is not available right now. Select a model again.") shows in the chat's own actions row only in the selected Agents wall window or a full chat pane; other wall windows only mark the chip. Picking a model clears it. The saved model on the session is not changed until the user picks.
- **Reply stats kept in the chat.** When a reply finishes, the host attaches `turnStats` to the reply's last assistant message before saving: provider, model, effort sent, input/output/cache tokens summed over every model call in the reply, decode time, wall time, model calls, tool steps and how it ended. The transcript shows them under the reply ("GPT Sol · ChatGPT · High effort · 14k in · 640 out · 80 tok/s · 21s", exact counts on hover), so a long chat shows which stretch ran on which model and where the tokens went. A reply that ended on tool calls gets its own stats row after its working steps. The floating status row keeps the live readout while streaming and the sleeping/blocked states, but no longer repeats finished numbers once they are on the reply, and the "Reply stats" placeholder is gone. The same totals and effort are added to `replies.jsonl` (counts only, no text) for later analysis. Daemon-driven runs keep the stats across transcript checkpoints. Incognito chats keep stats on their in-memory messages and write nothing to the reply log, as before. Replies from before this change have no stats and show none; nothing is backfilled or estimated.

### Hardware memory and subscription refresh accuracy

Implemented; focused tests, isolated wide/narrow light/dark renderer screenshots and theme-stable countdown frames verified: macOS Hardware memory excludes reclaimable file cache, using resident anonymous pages less purgeable pages, wired memory and the physical compressor footprint from the OS VM counters. Supports 4 KiB and 16 KiB pages. Probe failure reports unavailable rather than mislabeling cached memory. Other platforms retain existing measurements. Live host-versus-VM counter comparison is verified; exact Activity Monitor UI parity remains an unverified follow-up, not proof supplied by those shared-source counters. GiB versus Activity Monitor's GB labels can differ.

Mounted quota portfolios refresh every 60 seconds when idle and every 30 seconds for subscription accounts used by running chats. Reads are coalesced by account within the renderer; the host's 30-second throttle remains intact. Utilization shows a second-by-second “Refreshing in” countdown beside manual refresh, without claiming the provider's own accounting has updated. Disabled/closed portfolios stop scheduling. No new credentials or endpoints are used.

[Historical behavior, superseded by the 2026-10-13 background notification and panel batch above.] Budget savings remain all-time, independent of its spend/token time range, and the subscription label explicitly states this. Historical cache tokens use cache prices rather than full prompt prices; unknown models remain excluded with an unpriced-token warning. The reported earlier $2200 amount has not been reproduced from available historical records; no guessed price or fabricated balance is substituted. Follow-up requires the prior log/profile or evidence of the earlier calculation.

Quota consumer server rendering uses a stable idle live-run snapshot; full desktop regression coverage verifies React server rendering remains supported. Mounted synthetic-clock verification covers idle 60-second cadence, active 30-second cadence and return to idle after completion. Exact Activity Monitor UI parity and the original savings discrepancy remain unverified follow-ups. The requested scoped landing does not claim the historical $2200 has been recovered.

Quota polling subscribes only to running-session membership, not streamed-token versions, so token updates do not trigger quota consumer rendering. Membership and server-snapshot regression tests verify this distinction.

### Agent-window log access

Implemented; focused regression and isolated DOM routing verified: every transcript/full agent window retains its Logs action at compact sizes, using a drawn terminal icon with an accessible “Open agent logs” name when compact and a Logs label when roomy. The action opens the owning session's existing command log (`agent_<sessionId>`), not the selected chat's log, and does not start a shell or alter the conversation. The shared composer does not duplicate this header action. Matching light/dark wide/narrow before/after header screenshots were previously inspected and published; code-head 8954912 CI passes. Review handoff stays draft/unmerged by explicit request.

### Primary folder defaults and saved access revocation

Implemented, with focused automated regression coverage. Desktop interaction and visual evidence remain unverified in this batch.

- The last explicitly chosen primary folder is the default for new chats and is persisted locally across restarts. Explicit No folder is persisted too, including when a chat already has no primary. Supporting folders are not inherited by new chats.
- Refreshing settings or the folder list never replaces the current selection with the first saved folder. A saved default that is no longer registered does not grant access when creating a chat. Explicit No folder also prevents first-prompt auto-filing.
- Each saved folder in the composer picker has a separate X action labeled Revoke access. This removes saved workspace access and detaches that folder from saved chats, without deleting the directory, files, or chat worktrees. Removing a primary does not silently promote a supporting folder.
- The host rejects revocation while a running chat uses that folder as primary or supporting, and rejects primary/supporting selection mutations while the affected chat is running. Folder selection rejects IDs that are no longer registered. Revocation is not a filesystem permission boundary: independently attached paths and general tool permissions are unchanged.

### Image setup, local artifacts, and hardware refresh (2026-10-08)

Implemented, verification in progress: image chat checks runtime and supporting-file readiness before starting a turn, preserving the unsent prompt when setup is incomplete. Setup actions use plain-language Finish image setup / Download required files labels and explain that files are downloaded once while prompts stay local. Downloads remain explicit user actions, never automatic.

Folder-relative and Windows artifact links are recognized in Markdown. Explicit SVG image clicks open the existing isolated file viewer rather than returning Preview unavailable; SVG is not promoted to the raster data URL path. Complete text artifacts offer Download in the file viewer. Preview retains sandbox and network restrictions.

Empty space in the Agents title-bar toolbar remains draggable; interactive controls opt out individually. Hardware monitoring samples every two seconds instead of four, with a one-second GPU cache and existing in-flight GPU probe coalescing. This is device utilization, not per-model attribution.

Verification: desktop build and typecheck pass; 787 desktop tests pass, including generated relative SVG link coverage. Desktop drag motion, setup/download interactions, matched visual evidence, host regression checks, and cross-platform behavior are not yet verified.

### Local quota history and reply comparison

Implemented with focused automated verification: successful existing provider quota polls and fresh Claude response-header windows append numeric observations to `quota-history.jsonl` in the active host data directory. Records whitelist timestamp, configured provider id, source and window id/scope/used percentage/reset/status only; credentials, account identifiers, prompt content and provider bodies are never copied. Logging is best-effort and introduces no additional network polling. These account-wide observations are independent of transcripts, including incognito activity; incognito replies remain excluded from the reply log. History remains local until the user exports or removes it; no automatic retention limit is implemented.

New reply records include an explicit model-execution start timestamp alongside existing aggregate token counters. `node scripts/compare-quota.mjs [data-directory]` emits read-only JSON joining reply records with bracketing observations by configured provider. It shows total input processed, cache-hit rate where known, window percentage-point changes, sample gaps and other recorded replies in the observation interval. Unknown counters stay unknown. Missing samples and reset/decreased/window-changed observations never become claimed quota consumption. Account-wide changes may include other clients, incognito activity and delayed accounting; the command explicitly labels correlation, not exact chat billing. Older reply wall durations can supply approximate start times. No quota history can be reconstructed retroactively. Acceptance: isolated persistence/privacy tests, throttling/failure checks, missing counters, concurrent replies and reset handling. No visual change; a dashboard and exact per-request prompt-size logging remain unimplemented.

### Daemon sign-in ownership (PF14)

When the daemon owns the data directory, it is the single writer of tokens.json and handles desktop sign-in, CLI token imports, refresh and sign-out. Remote token exchanges and refreshes require HTTPS, including redirects; HTTP is permitted only in unit-test binaries for synthetic loopback providers. Browser callbacks bind loopback only. The web edition refuses desktop subscription sign-in. Synthetic flow and parity tests cover the implementation; live provider sign-in remains unverified before a release.

### Worktree setup output

New chat worktrees retain setup-command output through process exit. The daemon drains output readers to EOF within the original setup timeout and stops readers afterward, so an immediately exiting command cannot lose its final output. Exit status and failure details remain visible.

### Integrated local engine ownership (PF14)

With a configured data directory, the local engine owns sign-in tokens, providers, chat worktrees and admission, queued turns, context inspection, folder/index/memory/comment/spec/skill services, automation/workflows, watches, sandbox containers, runtime controls and the encrypted phone relay. Connector and index grounding for a normal reply is collected in that engine. Desktop, web and CLI use the same services; editions without that engine retain their host fallback. Offline chats continue to refuse remote endpoints and connector context. Sandbox replies still use the host executor while their container lifecycle is owned by the engine.

A chat preparing a reply admits only one send at a time. Stop cancels its preparation and setup command before inference begins, clears the preparation guard and lets a later send run. Setup output drains through process exit; cancellation stops the owned process tree. Production token exchanges require HTTPS, and sign-in callbacks bind loopback only. Activity decorations retain their frames and reduced-motion behavior while a shared 30 Hz clock lets the page rest between changes.

Live subscription sign-in and native phone-device/managed-relay verification remain follow-up acceptance work. Synthetic providers, a real loopback relay, isolated browser fixtures and a pinned local Docker image cover the integration without accessing real account credentials or device data.

Worktree routing compares canonical filesystem locations, including Windows short-name aliases and directory links. Existing chat mappings are repaired from the configured folder and saved source repository; a folder moved outside that repository is refused. Alias spelling must never route an isolated chat back into the shared source checkout.

## Windows installs without Visual Studio, and a smaller download (2026-10-12)

User intent: people hesitate when a Windows install seems to need Visual Studio. Nekko Agent never needs Visual Studio or any developer tool, and the install should be as small and as quiet as it can be.

- **Visual C++ runtime ships app-local.** `nekkod` links pyke's prebuilt ONNX Runtime, which is compiled against the DLL C runtime (`/MD`), so it imports `MSVCP140.dll` and `VCRUNTIME140.dll` and cannot link the runtime statically. The installer (and the `@nekko-agent/nekkod-win32-x64` npm package) carries the redistributable's DLLs beside `nekkod` (about 1 MB, copied from the build machine's Visual Studio redist folder by `scripts/vc-runtime.mjs`; packaging fails without them). Microsoft permits this app-local deployment.
- **Downloaded engines get it too.** llama.cpp's and stable-diffusion.cpp's Windows builds import the same runtime. When Windows lacks it (any of `msvcp140`, `vcruntime140`, `vcruntime140_1` missing from System32), the host copies the app's set beside the engine after an install and before every start, which also repairs earlier installs. When Windows has it, nothing is copied, so a newer system runtime is never shadowed.
- **A missing DLL is named, not mysterious.** An engine that exits with STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND reports that the Microsoft Visual C++ Runtime is missing, says Visual Studio is not needed, and links Microsoft's redistributable. A `nekkod` that Windows cannot load stops its restart loop and the desktop app falls back to the TS backend.
- **DirectML is fetched only on demand.** The 18 MB `DirectML.dll` is no longer packed. `nekkod` delay-loads it; a decision model's DirectML provider uses Windows' own copy when it is 1.15 or newer (Windows 11), else Microsoft's 1.15.4 fetched once from its NuGet package by HTTP range (about 9 MB, pinned SHA-256). Without either, decision models run on the CPU and the load message says why.
- **CUDA is preferred on NVIDIA cards that can run it.** llama.cpp's CUDA build is CUDA 13: it needs compute capability 7.5 (GTX 16 / RTX 20 series on) and driver 580 or newer, read once per run from `nvidia-smi --query-gpu=compute_cap,driver_version`. A supported card is recommended CUDA; an older card or driver is recommended Vulkan and the CUDA option says why; no reading keeps the earlier behavior (CUDA). The build picker tells people CUDA is roughly 15-20% faster than Vulkan on NVIDIA (a rough public figure; the benchmark on our hardware is open in TASKS.md). A supported NVIDIA machine already on a managed Vulkan or CPU build is offered **Switch to CUDA** on the Model server card.

Status: implemented. Verified locally: unit tests (host, desktop, server, scripts), workspace typecheck, the Rust crates' tests and clippy, a release `nekkod` staged by `stage-daemon.mjs` that lists `DirectML.dll` as a delay-load import and starts from a folder holding only it and the runtime DLLs, the DirectML range download against the live NuGet package (pinned hash matched, under a second), a `nekkod` with a deliberately broken import exiting 3221225781 and the desktop engine falling back to the TS backend, and headless before/after captures of the Model server card. Measured: the installer loses about 8.8 MB compressed (DirectML) and gains about 0.3 MB (runtime DLLs). `opt-level = "s"` would cut `nekkod` from 49 MB to 41 MB but only about 1.7 MB compressed, at a runtime cost to the daemon, so it is not applied. Not verified: a real install on a Windows PC without the Visual C++ Redistributable, DirectML fetched on an actual Windows 10 machine, the CUDA upgrade card clicked through on a live NVIDIA machine running Vulkan, a card older than compute capability 7.5, and the "roughly 15-20% faster" figure (T190).

### Agent plan visibility, context capacity and GPU identity

Implemented: plan-panel visibility is remembered locally per chat, so toggling one agent never changes another agent. The legacy saved preference remains the default for chats without their own choice. Narrow panes still fold their rails without changing the saved choice.

The context-window gauge has a subtle full background: green below 60%, yellow from 60% to below 85%, and red at 85% or higher. Its fill and sidebar context counts use the same thresholds. Unknown window limits remain neutral and token estimates retain their existing labels.

Hardware reports each detected GPU by its probe-reported device name, paired with that device's utilization instead of an unnamed peak. Missing utilization stays Unavailable, disabled monitoring stays Monitor off, and aggregate VRAM remains explicitly separate. No additional probes, permissions, provider requests or external data are introduced.

Verification: focused tests and isolated Windows Electron renderer checks pass, including two independent plan toggles, threshold boundaries and synthetic multi-GPU readings. Matched wide/narrow light/dark evidence captured; live multi-GPU hardware and macOS/Linux native app behavior are unverified.

### Agent panel group drag feedback

Implemented and verified in an isolated Windows Electron renderer at desktop/mobile widths, both orientations and light/dark themes, with synthetic drag events and inspected captures. Physical OS dragging and macOS/Linux runtime verification remain untested. Dragging a project group opens a quick 140 ms insertion gap before the hovered group, moving its contents down (right in the horizontal panel), without an orange overlay. Moving outside closes the gap; crossing children does not reset hover. Reduced-motion preferences disable transitions. Dropping over a group header or its agent cards saves the project order; dropping on itself leaves order unchanged. Agent-card reordering and moving agents between projects remain separate operations. This changes local layout/settings only and does not start agents or contact providers.

## Slim wave rail, tighter Agents spacing, composer snap points

The rail colour and gutters below are historical and superseded by the compact-board update above.

[Implemented 2026-10-14; renderer captures inspected in an isolated hidden fixture; native macOS window not checked] The left nav rail is 52px wide. Its icons are centred vertically on a faded-accent band (accent mixed 14% into the paper colour, 20% when expanded) whose top and bottom curve back into the window's left edge within ~14% of each end. Hovering or keyboard focus expands it to 188px with a spring width transition and the labels/items sliding in; reduced motion disables the transition. On macOS the title bar leaves 92px (was 78px) before the app mark so it clears the traffic lights with breathing room. On the Agents page the gaps between rail, agent list, agent window and the right edge shrink (page padding 4/6px, list-to-window 2px, wall-to-dock 6px), widening the agent window from 644 to 746px at 1440×900 with the dock open. The right panel dock defaults to 368px (about 10% narrower). The composer height drag, in both the chat pane and the Agents wall composer, snaps to the default (content-fitted) height and to full height when released within 24px of either; snapping to default clears the saved height. No settings shape, host action or permission changes.

The empty-wall picker remains available, anchored beside New terminal. Existing sessions, saved layouts and permissions are unchanged. ### Repository-row creation controls

Implemented: the Agents panel repository-row plus creates a multimodal agent chat and the terminal icon creates a shell using the clicked repository, without changing the globally selected repository or saved default folders. A new window becomes visible in Focus without changing layout mode; an incompatible wall filter resets to All so the result is not hidden. Composer agent selection is independent of a terminal being the Focus hero. On narrow screens the agent-list overlay closes after successful creation, but stays open on failure. Both row controls remain disabled for that repository while creation is pending; failures show a repository-specific error toast and allow retry. Existing session configuration cleanup remains in place.

Acceptance: one host creation per pending repository action, explicit repository payloads even when another repository/default is selected, existing wall panes retained, visible Focus results, Grid/Dynamic mode preserved, and error feedback on host failures. Verification uses synthetic host responses, isolated profiles and network-disabled off-screen non-focusable Electron windows. Real host terminal spawning and non-macOS desktop behavior remain unverified; these controls do not themselves run agent prompts or write repository files.
